# ERP_MCP decision / evidence log

Append-only. One dated entry per durable decision, evidence or refusal that future work needs.

## 2026-10-06 — Freeze E2E acceptance contract before product changes

- Decision: `docs/E2E_ACCEPTANCE_CONTRACT.md` freezes U01–U18 and A01–A54 (actor, action,
  expected, negative, evidence, class) before any product fix or E2E implementation, so the
  acceptance surface cannot move during implementation. Changes need a new plan amendment.
- Decision: tracked closing documents record `CODE_EVIDENCE_SHA` and local counts and mark hosted
  CI as pending; the exact final head SHA and hosted run IDs are recorded only in the PR #11
  body/comment (a tracked file cannot contain its own commit SHA or the CI run of that commit).
- Evidence: baseline with PostgreSQL 16 + Redis 7 locally: 1157 passed, 6 skipped (previous
  checkpoint: 39 skips). Remaining skips: 3 private-Ferma, 1 real-1C OData, 1 live-RSV (now run
  and passing locally), 1 native lifecycle (passes via `scripts.rsv_native_lifecycle_harness`).
- Refusal: RSV `reveal`, `execute_query` and generic business query stay disabled (documented
  privacy/company-scope/upstream-bound constraints are not remediated).

## 2026-10-06 - Disposable local E2E environment (branch e2e/local-environment)

- Decision: gateway, Fake1C and the test IdP run as host processes (uvicorn from the worktree
  venv); only PostgreSQL 16 and Redis 7 run in compose project `erpmcp-e2e`. Rationale: identical
  issuer/audience URLs for browser and gateway, trivial stop/start fault injection; the
  production gateway image is already built and scanned in CI.
- Decision: secrets are generated randomly once under the git-ignored `.e2e/`; the test IdP is
  the only place identities live. ERP_MCP gained no user-creation path and no 1C write path.
- Evidence: see docs/E2E_ENVIRONMENT.md and the commit history of this branch.

## 2026-10-07 - E2E test scaffolding

- Decision: tests/e2e is auto-skipped only when .e2e/env.json is absent and ERP_MCP_E2E_NO_SKIP is unset; scripts/e2e/test.ps1 sets it so a missing environment is an error.
- Evidence: 12 smoke tests green against the live environment (tests/e2e/test_smoke_environment.py).

## 2026-10-07 - Data-plane User E2E suite U01-U18 (branch e2e/user-flows)

- Decision: Fake1C (testbed/fake1c/app.py) now records every request (method, path, user agent,
  numeric $top/$skip, credentials-present flag; no header values) at `/__ft__/requests`, the same
  shape as the Functional Tester recorder, so U04/U06/U11/U13/U14 can prove "only GET/HEAD" and
  "no upstream request on denial". The fake also honours `$select`/`$skip` like real OData.
- Finding (needs GPT-PM contract clarification): the baseline seed grants UC1 a company-one grant
  only, but source-level tools (source_health, onec_*) require a source-wide grant in the registry
  (`g.company_id IS NULL`), and a source-wide grant also covers company two. U02-U04/U11-U14 therefore
  run under a temporary source-wide grant created/revoked through the Admin API; U05-U10 use the
  baseline company-scoped grants. The denial under the company-only grant is asserted explicitly.
- Evidence: collect-only and ruff clean; not yet executed (environment owned by lane A).
- U05-U10 written under the baseline seed. Company row-content assertions skip with the explicit reason `BLOCKED-no-validated-semantic-profile` (a validated profile needs native reconciliation evidence, intentionally not faked); access decisions are proven by the `ACCESS_AUTHORIZED` audit receipt and the Fake1C request log. U08 removes the group at the IdP by editing the git-ignored `.e2e/idp-config.json` and restarting the test IdP, restoring the original bytes in `finally`.
- U11-U18 written. Outage rows (U14-U16) use scripts/e2e/fault.ps1 inside `outage()` whose `finally` always restarts the component; recovery is polled on an observable condition (a successful call), the gateway is never restarted. Observed contract recorded in the tests: with Redis down the rate-limited source/company tools fail closed while system_status/sources_list (no limiter) keep working; onec_read bounds rows by clamping `$top` to max_rows and exposes no truncation flag. U18 runs `pytest tests/functional` with FT_* wired from .e2e/env.json, or skips with `FT-suite-not-merged`.
- Lint-only follow-up: removed an unused noqa in testbed/fake1c/app.py (ruff RUF100).
- 2026-10-07 acceptance: clean up/status/smoke/reset/smoke/up/fault/down all executed; service launch changed to cmd.exe ShellExecute so callers reading output pipes do not hang. check_document_consistency reports a stale engineering checkpoint (implementation fingerprint covers scripts/tests/testbed); refresh is left to the integrator after merge.
## 2026-10-07 — Test-only synthetic fixture semantic profiles (SC public coverage, step 1)

- Decision: reviewed synthetic fixture profiles are a code-level provider
  (`src/business_ai_gateway/fixture_profiles.py`), never a `bag.semantic_profiles` row, never
  VALIDATED, no migration. The ten-native-reports rule (DB CHECK 006, admin validate, runtime
  `require_usable_semantic_profile`) is untouched; a real VALIDATED row always wins, and any
  non-retired DB profile for the same source/company/concept blocks the fixture (no fall-through).
- Hard deny layers (each tested): (1) `Settings` rejects the file/sha settings in production,
  when incomplete, or when `BAG_ENVIRONMENT != test`; (2) provider constructor and every lookup
  require environment `test`, and `Registry(production=True, synthetic_profiles=...)` is refused;
  (3) the source must be listed in the pinned file AND carry tag `synthetic-fixture`; (4) a file
  SHA-256 mismatch fails `Runtime` startup. Open decision resolved: `development` is NOT permitted;
  the functional stack must run with `BAG_ENVIRONMENT=test`.
- Provenance: every semantic tool response now carries `profile_kind`, `evidence_level`,
  `native_reconciliation`, `warnings`; fixture responses are `SYNTHETIC_FIXTURE`/`L1`/`NOT_RUN` with
  warning `SYNTHETIC_FIXTURE_PROFILE_NOT_NATIVE`, fingerprint prefix `synthetic-fixture:` and
  success audit `detail_code=SYNTHETIC_FIXTURE_PROFILE`.
- Deviations from the design (code contradicted it): (a) `register_read` and the register
  capability evidence required by every profile tool need the OData sidecar; plain Fake1C cannot
  serve them, so a test-only protocol double `testbed/fake1c/sidecar_app.py`
  (`business_ai_gateway.testbed.fake_sidecar`) was added; (b) Fake1C ignored `$filter/$orderby/
  $select/$skip`; it now evaluates the conjunctive filters the gateway builds (read-only); (c) the
  fixture pins the metadata fingerprint only; capability evidence is bound to it by
  `require_profile_capabilities`. Re-pin with `scripts/synthetic_fixture_profiles.py --write`.

## 2026-10-07 - SC step 2: Fake1C data for SC04/SC05/SC07/SC09 and seed consistency

- Decision: Fake1C seed gains dates/counterparty/currency on Document_Sales/Purchases, an unposted sale for organization one (SALE-003), a backdated posted sale (SALE-004, 2025-12-31) with posting rows only for posted documents in a new AccountingRegister_Ledger, inventory item 002 (receipt 10, return recorded as an expense row 3, balance 7), an earlier cash receipt (93, 2026-03-15) so the 2026-03-01..05-01 window nets 100 = scenario fixture, and the inventory balance of item 001 corrected 7 -> 5 to equal its movements. No migration, no new tool, no return direction.
- Consequence for testers: SC11 April inventory window now contains two items; per-item net (item 001 = 5) is the SC11 invariant and item 002 net 7 equals its Balance (SC05).

## 2026-10-07 - SC step 3: SC10 account turnover on Fake1C

- Decision: the fake sidecar serves a read-only `balanceAndTurnovers` virtual table for `AccountingRegister_Ledger` (capability evidence carries the live metadata fingerprint; sidecar `virtual_tables` is overridable so a missing capability is testable). Seed row 100 + 40 - 15 = 125 for organization one; other companies get no rows; naive timestamps are rejected by the existing tool validator. No metadata or migration change.

## 2026-10-07 - SC step 4: receivable_aging / payable_aging (SC01-SC03)

- Decision: two new read-only tools reuse capability keys ar.read/ap.read (no policy migration) and the existing aging engine; new concepts receivable.open_items / payable.open_items validated by `settlement_collector.py` (deviation: validator lives there, not semantic.py, to keep semantic.py unchanged) and wired into the shared registry validator chain and scripts/semantic_profiles.py. Fixture covers receivable only; payable has no fixture profile and is fail-closed. validated_profiles is derived server-side, never from tool input; evidence level is L1 for fixtures, mapping-declared for native profiles. Fake1C gains AccumulationRegister_SettlementItems (additive wiring); company-two item proves scope isolation.
- SC06 stays EXTERNAL-GATE and SC08 NOT IMPLEMENTED (out of freeze), unchanged.
- Executed against the live environment (up.ps1 -Seed baseline, then test.ps1 -Suite user): 41 passed, 3 skipped (U05/U06 row-content `BLOCKED-no-validated-semantic-profile` x2, U18 `FT-suite-not-merged`), 0 failed. One test defect fixed: U12 expected CamelCase exception names but the audit detail_code is `SEMANTIC_PROFILE_UNVALIDATED`. Observation: the MCP client sees only the generic text "Error executing tool <name>" for every failure; specific codes are visible only in audit rows. The pre-existing lane-A PostgreSQL volume `erpmcp-e2e-pgdata` (old secrets) was removed with `down.ps1 -Purge` to initialise this worktree's own secrets.
# Decision log (functional-tester branch qa/functional-tester-sc01-sc12)

## 2026-10-07 - Black-box functional suite for SC01-SC12

- Decision: author tests/functional (real MCP Streamable-HTTP client, env-parametrised FT_*) and a
  private disposable stack (scripts/ft, ports 25432/26379/28766/28000). No src/, migration or
  security-boundary change.
- Decision: statuses are observed at run time. A scenario is PASS only if a public tool answered and
  the invariant held; otherwise EXTERNAL-GATE (tool fails closed: no VALIDATED semantic profile, and
  bag.semantic_profiles requires ten native-report cases by DB check constraint) or NOT IMPLEMENTED
  (no public tool/data). No synthetic profile or native evidence was fabricated.
- Evidence: Fake1C is synthetic L1 only; never native 1C reconciliation.
- Commit: see git log on this branch.

## 2026-10-07 - Dispositions and token default

- SC10 args now timezone-qualified (tester bug). SC06 encoded EXTERNAL-GATE (VAT only when validated), SC08 NOT IMPLEMENTED (outside frozen scope; needs operator rebaseline). FT_BEARER_TOKEN is now the default identity for every call.

## 2026-10-07 - Suite moved onto the fixture-profile stack

- Stack now BAG_ENVIRONMENT=test with the reviewed synthetic fixture profile file (SHA-256 pinned at setup), source tag synthetic-fixture, test-only fake sidecar behind a recording wrapper (:28767). SC01-SC05, SC07, SC09-SC12 evaluate real tool output (PASS, L1 synthetic only); SC06 EXTERNAL-GATE, SC08 NOT IMPLEMENTED by decision. Final run 73 passed, 3 skipped (needs-oidc-identity), 2 xfailed (SC06, SC08).

## 2026-10-07 - Review remediation F1-F6 (aging fail-closed, honest audit)

- Decision: the sidecar client `read` now returns the envelope `page`; receivable/payable aging treats `page.truncated` (any non-False value) as INCONCLUSIVE/AGING_ROWS_TRUNCATED with no partial rows. Other semantic tools already echo `result["page"]`, so they now expose the flag without further change.
- Decision: float amounts with more than 15 significant digits are rejected as SETTLEMENT_FACT_INVALID instead of rounded (chosen over parse_float=Decimal to avoid changing numeric typing of every other tool). Row parsing catches only OpenItemsInvalid (unhashable record types are now bad data); evaluate_aging/scope/profile errors propagate and audit as outcome=error. A response without a `value` list is INCONCLUSIVE/SOURCE_RESPONSE_INVALID, never an empty PASS.
- Decision: audit vocabulary is unchanged (success/error/denied; no migration). COMPANY_SCOPE_MISMATCH, SETTLEMENT_FACT_INVALID, AGING_ROWS_TRUNCATED and SOURCE_RESPONSE_INVALID audit as `error` (closest existing non-success value; `denied` is reserved for authorization). Non-conclusive aging detail_code is `<profile marker>:<reason>` (e.g. SYNTHETIC_FIXTURE_PROFILE:AGING_ROWS_TRUNCATED); exception-path audit also keeps the marker and profile fingerprint.
- Decision: profile_provenance raises PROFILE_PROVENANCE_UNKNOWN unless profile_kind is VALIDATED_NATIVE or SYNTHETIC_FIXTURE.

## 2026-10-07 - Review remediation F7: test doubles out of the production image

- Decision: `.dockerignore` gains `**/testbed` so `src/business_ai_gateway/testbed/` (Fake1C, fake sidecar) is not in the production build context; the root `testbed` entry only matched the top-level directory. No production module imports the package (static test asserts this and the Dockerfile COPY lines). Scripts copied into the image that import it (fanout_load_drill, synthetic_fixture_profiles) are CI/test tools and are not run from the image; the CI image smoke only imports business_ai_gateway.app.

## 2026-10-07 - Review remediation F8: product chain tests cannot silently skip

- Decision: the four product chain modules share `tests/sc_stack.py` PG_MARKS/needs_pg; with ERP_MCP_REQUIRE_DB_TESTS=1 and no BAG_PRIVILEGE_TEST_DATABASE_URL they fail (fixture `require_pg_database`) instead of skipping; CI sets the variable on the pytest step. Assertions strengthened: naive-timestamp tests assert error detail/no audit row and no upstream read, SC04 asserts the exact audit rows, stale-fingerprint and invalid-VALIDATED-row tests assert no upstream read; dead `deltas` line removed.

## 2026-10-07 - Review remediation: fallout and checkpoint re-pin

- Decision: test doubles in tests/test_server_audit.py that stand in for the registry now carry `profile_kind: VALIDATED_NATIVE` like the real registry (required by the stricter profile_provenance); SC04 audit assertions match the exact rows (access row plus tool row); engineering checkpoint fingerprint and report markers re-pinned to the new implementation content. Full suite with own PG/Redis and ERP_MCP_REQUIRE_DB_TESTS=1: 1189 passed, 140 skipped (live-stack e2e/functional suites and the 6 env-gated tests).
## 2026-10-07 - Admin E2E suite A01-A54 (branch e2e/admin-flows)

- Decision: the Admin suite runs on seed mode bootstrap-only; source, companies, role bindings and grants are created through /admin/ by the `world` fixture (tests/e2e/admin/admin_support.py). The suite fails loudly (never skips) when the environment is not pristine.
- Decision: contract rows whose evidence the product does not currently produce (login/logout audit rows for A01, audit trail for 401 denials in A03/A04) are asserted strictly in their own tests so a failure is visible and classified, not weakened.
- Evidence: batches so far (A01-A54) collect cleanly and are ruff-clean; executed next against the live environment.
- Finding (live run 1, 62 passed / 20 failed): Admin UI inline onclick handlers are blocked by the page CSP script-src self, so Create grant, Sign out, dialog Submit/Cancel and Retry do nothing in a real browser; tests isolate this in test_A45_ui_actions_are_not_blocked_by_the_page_csp and open dialogs programmatically for other rows.
- Finding: profile validation cannot succeed locally without register-capability evidence (no sidecar): CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED; validation-success tests report this as an environment limitation.
- Evidence (live, seed bootstrap-only, two consecutive full runs from a reset environment): 76 passed / 12 failed / 12 deselected(smoke) in 459s and 528s with an identical failure set; every failure is a strict product-defect or environment-limitation test (audit gaps A01/A03/A16, broken metadata accepted A39, inline onclick blocked by CSP A45/A49, focus loss A46, same-dialog retry 409 A50, no register-capability evidence A06/A40).

FT_BEARER_TOKEN* wired from the IdP; uc1 has a temporary source-wide grant for the run. ## 2026-10-07 - Admin defect fix P4 (branch fix/admin-defects)

- Decision: `parse_metadata` rejects well-formed XML that is not OData `$metadata` (root must be Edmx with Schema/EntityType/EntitySet). An HTML 200 for `$metadata` therefore yields metadata_supported=false and no fingerprint, so the admin refresh fails with an audited error and the previous STABLE evidence is untouched (A39). Regression: tests/test_metadata.py, tests/test_compatibility.py.

## 2026-10-07 - Admin defect fix P8 (branch fix/admin-defects)

- Decision: server-side fix, UI keeps its key (scripts/verify_admin_ui.py already requires key retention on retry). A prior idempotency outcome of `error` (domain write rolled back; only reservation and audit row persisted) with the same actor+command+payload fingerprint is re-armed to `pending` and re-executed; success still replays, different payload/command still 409 (A29/A30), concurrent callers serialize on the row lock. Regression: tests/test_admin_mutations_postgres.py::test_failed_attempt_is_retryable_with_the_same_key_and_payload. No migration.

## 2026-10-07 - Admin defect fix P1/P2/P3 audit gaps (branch fix/admin-defects)

- Decision: no CHECK constraint exists on bag.admin_audit_events.action (only outcome IN success/error/denied/conflict), so no migration. New action values: `session.login`, `session.logout` (A01, verified subject/client from the session; client_id stored in the Redis session record), `auth.denied` (A03/A04), `source.probe` (A16; actor, target host only, outcome, no URL or secrets).
- Decision (flood control): a rejected bearer is audited only when it is JWT-shaped (3 base64url segments, <=8 KiB) and at most 30 rows/minute/process; the row uses actor `unverified` and carries no token material or unverified claims. Plain garbage, scanners and missing credentials leave no row. Audit write failures never change the 401/probe response.
- Regression: tests/test_admin_session.py (login/logout audit, bounded denial audit).

## 2026-10-07 - Admin defect fix P5/P6 (branch fix/admin-defects)

- Decision (P5): all inline `on*` attributes removed from the admin UI; controls carry `data-act` and a single delegated click listener dispatches through an `ACTIONS` registry (functions looked up at call time because several are overridden). CSP is unchanged (script-src 'self', no unsafe-inline). Static guards: tests/test_admin_ui_asset.py (no on* attribute, every data-act registered) and scripts/verify_admin_ui.py (same regex plus the real CSP header on the served page, console CSP violations fail the run).
- Decision (P6): after a render, if focus fell to <body> (route change replaced the nav button, or a closed dialog's opener was re-rendered) focus moves to the main h1 (tabindex -1); a failed Submit restores focus to the previously focused control inside the dialog after the buttons are re-enabled.

## 2026-10-07 - Admin defect fixes: verification and checkpoint re-pin (branch fix/admin-defects)

- Evidence: full pytest with own postgres:16-alpine + redis:7-alpine and ERP_MCP_REQUIRE_DB_TESTS=1: 1196 passed, 228 skipped (baseline 1189 + 7 new tests; skips are env-gated e2e/functional/private); ruff, compileall, scripts/verify_admin_ui.py (real Chromium, page CSP enforced) and check_document_consistency pass. test_admin_api double updated for the new `mutations` attribute used by the rejected-bearer audit. Engineering checkpoint fingerprint and report markers re-pinned.
- Not verified live: the live admin E2E suite could not start because the shared compose volume erpmcp-e2e-pgdata holds a password from an earlier environment (InvalidPasswordError in envctl ensure-roles); fixing it needs `down.ps1 -Purge` (volume deletion), left to the operator. Note: a leftover ignored .e2e/ directory makes the unit suite activate the e2e fixtures and fail massively; keep it out of the worktree when running the full suite.

## 2026-10-07 - Admin E2E on candidate d493edb: capability refusal mapping, row labels, declared EXTERNAL-GATE

- Decision: CapabilityUnsupported raised inside an Admin mutation (e.g. profile validate without register-capability evidence) is a domain refusal and now maps to HTTP 409 with the unchanged code CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED instead of HTTP 500 ADMIN_DEPENDENCY_FAILED; unmapped exceptions still map to sanitized 500. Regression test: tests/test_admin_api.py::test_capability_refusal_is_a_conflict_not_a_server_fault. No gate was weakened.
- Decision: A06 validate/scope-mapping rows assert the PLATFORM_ADMIN is authorized (4xx domain refusal, never 200/201, audited as error not denied) while the read-only role gets 403; native reconciliation evidence is never fabricated. A40 half "validated profile turns stale on drift" is an explicit EXTERNAL-GATE skip with the exact reason "EXTERNAL-GATE: validated profile requires native reconciliation evidence", the single entry of ALLOWED_SKIP_REASONS in tests/e2e/skip_policy.py (policy test updated).
- Decision: Admin UI row action buttons share visible labels (Edit / disable, Revoke); each now has a row-specific aria-label that starts with the visible label (WCAG 2.5.3). A45 "keyboard trap" was a test defect: the trap detector compared labels, so adjacent identical-label row buttons looked like one element; it now compares DOM index and position.
- Decision: A38 expects the EntitySet count of the live Fake1C $metadata instead of a hard-coded 10 (the product lane extended Fake1C to 12).
- Evidence: unit/static tests for the 409 mapping and the aria-labels pass; the live admin rerun result is recorded below once executed.
- Evidence: admin suite live on candidate d493edb + these changes, two consecutive runs from a reset environment: 87 passed, 1 skipped (declared EXTERNAL-GATE), 0 failed (470 s and 465 s); engineering checkpoint re-pinned to implementation_sha256 3efb2f8f; document consistency PASS.

## 2026-10-07 - GPT-PM sprint-end review of 56a202f: REJECT (2 findings, both verified and fixed)

- Finding 1 (BLOCKER, verified): hosted run 37563391510 on 56a202f FAILED in check_report_references.py with "stale engineering checkpoint". Cause: implementation_fingerprint normalised CRLF only for a fixed extension list that has no .ps1; the Windows working tree holds CRLF PowerShell scripts (scripts/e2e, scripts/ft) while Linux hashes LF. Decision: normalise CRLF to LF for every file without a NUL byte; added regression tests (LF vs CRLF for .ps1/.sh/.sql/.css/no-extension, binary untouched); implementation_sha256 re-pinned; the fingerprint of a `git archive` export (Linux-equivalent content) must equal the recorded one. The failed run is a real failure, not a non-run, and is not used as evidence.
- Finding 2 (MAJOR, verified): the frozen contract said U18 needs all twelve SC PASS, while the U18 oracle accepted SC06 and SC08 as expected xfail without a formal amendment. Decision: Amendment A1 in docs/E2E_ACCEPTANCE_CONTRACT.md records the dispositions (SC06 EXTERNAL-GATE: validated profile; SC08: duplicate-counterparty detection is not a committed lane of the scope freeze, needs an operator rebaseline) and U18 is reported everywhere as "10 of 12 PASS + 2 declared dispositions, not 12/12". No scope was expanded and SC08 was not implemented. The amendment takes effect only with GPT-PM approval.

## 2026-10-07 - SC08 sprint-end local reviewers: findings triaged

- Three local sonnet-medium reviewers (security, silent-failure, test adequacy) ran on the uncommitted SC08 diff. No BLOCKER. Closed in code: catalog `Ref_Key` and the company ref are compared as canonical GUIDs (contract section 4 "compared case-insensitively as GUIDs"; a non-GUID/empty catalog `Ref_Key` is COUNTERPARTY_FACT_INVALID, a braced/hyphenless company ref no longer yields a false COMPANY_SCOPE_MISMATCH); a lone surrogate in `Ref_Key`/`Code`/`Description` is COUNTERPARTY_FACT_INVALID instead of an unhandled UnicodeEncodeError; `source.entity_allowed()` is checked for BOTH planned entity sets before the first upstream read (audited `denied`, zero reads); the serialised response is bounded by `max_response_bytes` (fixed code RESPONSE_TOO_LARGE, audited `error`, no data) because NFKC can expand a match_key. None changes detection semantics for valid data, so no Amendment A3.
- Closed in tests: digit-only GUID fixtures made case tests vacuous (now hex-letter GUIDs), member-sort/zero-GUID/normalisation survivors, server wiring (top clamp, exact upstream kwargs, exact audit query, response echo), live-metadata gate, bad DB mapping, non-truncation INCONCLUSIVE audit outcomes, caplog privacy check, U18 requires the named SC08 cases.
- ACCEPTED LIMITATION (reviewer MAJOR, silent-failure F1): an activity read that returns zero usable rows (wrong-but-valid `external_ref`, or only null counterparties) yields PASS `NO_DUPLICATE_CANDIDATES`, indistinguishable from a full scan with no duplicates. Contract sections 6/8 define exactly this and the response always carries `human_review_required: true`; fixing it needs a new public field or reason (Amendment A3 + GPT-PM approval). Not done in this sprint; surfaced to the GPT-PM end review as a known item and pinned by a test documenting the current behaviour.
- Accepted/no change: the catalog has no company dimension (a counterparty active in several companies is visible to a caller of any one of them) per contract; capability/$metadata probes precede the profile gate (contract "zero reads" means zero entity-data reads); a bad row anywhere in the catalog makes the whole scan INCONCLUSIVE (fail-closed availability coupling); with default max_rows=200 any real catalog >= 200 rows is INCONCLUSIVE (contract section 7).

## 2026-10-07 - Real 1C 818HA lane: runner, evidence rules and observed product findings

- Decision: the lane code (`scripts/real1c`, `tests/real1c`) is committed strictly after the frozen catalogue commit `83341b7`. The acceptance run executes in gate mode only on a committed lane tree; `--dev` runs (dirty tree, replay of cached observations) are never acceptance evidence. The runner stops before any story on a failed gate: catalogue sha256, reference manifest hash, testbed readiness, live COM fingerprint equal to the manifest, stack reachable. After the run the COM fingerprint must equal the pre-run one, and every generated output is swept for literal secrets and credential patterns before it is written (a hit aborts the write).
- Disposition rules (mechanical, `scripts/real1c/disposition.py`): PASS only for class RR with a real oracle match; semantic data tools behind an unvalidated profile are reported SEMANTIC_PROFILE_UNVALIDATED, never PASS; NATIVE_COM_QUERY comparisons (NR-01..NR-10, 21 invoices, raw-L2) are context only and give EVIDENCE_REQUIRED at best, because the genuine native UI report is still owed by the owner. The 180 month-close rules are dispositioned one by one: CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED (no rule engine in the public surface) or EVIDENCE_REQUIRED (documents required), with COM account applicability as supplementary context.
- Test adapters (not product behaviour): a loopback fault/recording proxy sits between the sidecar and the real 1C publication (GET/HEAD only, counts methods, injects slow/down/drift/truncate). It carries a HEAD-to-GET shim because the real 1C answers `HEAD $metadata` with 405 while the gateway health check uses HEAD; the shim is stated in the report, and SYS-01 records the raw 405 as a product finding. The real Node sidecar runs from a host build with its own launcher (`lane_start.mjs`), because `server.mjs` does not start on Windows.
- Finalizer changes made in memory only for this testbed: reader secret saved as a DPAPI CurrentUser blob (win32crypt), narrowed unsafe-role token for the reader, probe CreateItem classification, a BSP "Пользователи" item linked to the reader on both clones (reference/probe). The immutable golden DT was never modified; write probes ran only on the probe clone inside a rolled-back transaction.
- Observed product findings (evidence in `reports/real1c/case_results.json`; triage is an operator decision, none is silently fixed here): (1) `onec_read` paging with `$top/$skip` and no `$orderby` double-counts and skips rows on the real 1C (RL2-10); (2) `HEAD $metadata` is rejected with 405 by the real publication (SYS-01); (3) some malformed calls (naive `as_of`, `top=0` on aging tools) are rejected without an audit row (SYS-02, ST-076); (4) the product does not report the privilege class of its upstream 1C identity (SYS-03, ST-085); (5) `companies_list` has no paging arguments (SYS-04, ST-075); (6) time-boxed grants already exist, so ST-077 catalogued as unsupported is a catalogue correction; (7) `onec_read` is source-scoped, a source-level grant is not limited to one organisation (RL2-09 note).
- Open operator decisions: COM vs native UI as the meaning of "native"; REAL_1C_SEED_SPEC "synthetic only" vs DAD designating 818HA as the reference base; which findings become roadmap items; the owner still has to produce at least ten genuine native UI reports; manual acceptance PENDING; hosted CI on the exact head PENDING; production NO-GO; PR #11 stays Draft.

- Finding (GPT-PM review of the local head, MAJOR): the generated artifacts contained exact accounting values (turnovers, balances, invoice and payment totals) and amount literals inside the COM query text, while the lane claimed that business values are never stored; the credential sweep could not see them. eal1c_e2e\private_evidence\`). The final output sweep now also searches every public file for each exact figure of the private results, so a sanitizer regression aborts the write. The earlier local commit that carried unsanitized reports was never pushed and was replaced (soft reset of an unpushed commit), so those values never reached the remote history.

The running L2 stand had its two fake processes stopped: the gateway answered the same 521.1 balance question from the real 1C without them. Not exercised live: a fresh `up.ps1` run in the new profile (static parse and unit tests only).
- Finding for the operator: the 818HA configuration has no accumulation register of settlements with counterparties among its 76 accumulation registers, and the published accounting-register record carries no counterparty sub-account fields, so `payable_balance`/`receivable_balance` may not be mappable as designed; a mapping or design decision is needed.

- Sprint-end local review (security, test adequacy; sonnet-medium) and its triage: closed in this batch: the preflight now treats only a genuine rights refusal as denial (other errors are INCONCLUSIVE and refuse the run); the start primitive of the E2E harness refuses Fake1C/sidecar in the real profile (also via fault.ps1); outputs must be under the private evidence root and are re-checked after mkdir; the probe clone must not resolve to the reference clone; connect errors are wrapped without their text; a manifest without a baseline or a reader that can fingerprint no table is refused; env.ps1 values are quote-escaped; the evidence basis states exactly what was compared; tests added for each. - FINDING (operator decision): the reader ERP_MCP_TEST_READER is not write-free. A read-only AccessRight sweep of the reference clone shows Insert on 32 catalogs, Update on 33 catalogs, 3 accumulation registers and 19 information registers, Delete on 1 catalog (HR attached files, report variants and user report settings, HR classifiers); no Documents, AccountingRegisters or ChartsOfAccounts, no Administration right. The lane's zero-write evidence (GET/HEAD only upstream, identical fingerprints) stands, but the earlier statement that the identity is write-denied rests on one probe object. The generator records the sweep and refuses a reader that may write ledgers; trimming the reader role on the clone is an operator decision.
- Hosted CI on 48aad2f: the `test` job failed on one test that assumed a Windows drive path (fixed: skipped off Windows); the `odata-upstream` job failed in the Trivy image scan of the pinned OData sidecar, which this PR does not touch (no sidecar, Dockerfile or workflow change); a fresh vulnerability database is the likely cause and the finding list was not available in the job log. Recorded for GPT-PM and the operator; PR #11 is not re-run.
- Sprint-end GPT-PM REJECT on 300d2a5 (3 MAJORs) and remediation. - Hybrid OData + COM analytics balance (operator decision 2026-10-07; GPT-PM plan APPROVE `erp_mcp-integration-candidate-2026-10-07T14-18-25-002Z-789f07` after a first REJECT with two MAJORs that were accepted: parity proof must bind the OData publication and the COM connection to one disposable clone, and the route must be chosen before execution with no runtime OData-to-COM fallback and an exactly source-bound binding). Branch `feature/hybrid-analytics-route` from cb05aba. Stage 1: ADR-0008 and the threat-model delta. Evidence that changed the design: live read-only GETs as the reader on the real publication show `Balance(Period=..., AccountCondition=..., Condition=...)` of `AccountingRegister_Хозрасчетный` returns `Account_Key`, `ExtDimension1..3` with types and split balances; `AccountCondition` accepts one or an OR-joined list of `Account_Key eq guid''...''`; filtering the chart of accounts by `Code` is refused by the reader on this publication, so account keys are pinned in the profile. The earlier audit claim that this route does not exist was wrong and is corrected. Scope-freeze: the COM fallback is an explicit operator rebaseline for this one capability (ADR-0008 §9).
- Stage 1 document set of the hybrid route: ADR-0008, threat-model section, document index entry, dashboard re-render (document consistency PASS). Stages 2-6 follow on the same branch, one commit per stage.
- Hybrid route stage 2-3 (gateway): concept `account.balance_by_analytics`, mapping validator, route selector, OData route and tool `accounting_balance_by_analytics(source_id, company_id, as_of)` (scope `accounting.read`, three parameters). Route is decided before execution from the persisted capability profile for the exact metadata fingerprint: AVAILABLE -> odata (bindings never consulted); proven absent (sidecar evidence kind `metadata-function-import-absent-or-not-read-only`) with an exactly matching approved binding -> com; UNKNOWN or stale/drifted -> fail closed with no source call; any error of the selected route fails closed and never switches route. Amounts use the split debit/credit fields as Decimal. Binding file is loaded from a pinned sha256 (same pattern as the evidence index). Tests: tests/test_analytics_balance_mapping.py, tests/test_analytics_balance_routing.py, discovery list updated.
- Hybrid route stage 4-5 (COM bridge, parity): separate loopback bridge package `onec_com_bridge/` (bearer token, strict request model with no text/path/secret fields, exact binding match, company allow-list before any COM call, one fixed code-owned query template, reader identity from a DPAPI secret file, row cap, sanitized errors), gateway client, parity harness and runbook `docs/runbooks/COM_BRIDGE_AND_PARITY.md`. LIVE checks on disposable clones only (reader, read-only, reference clone never opened): (1) the COM connector has no `Type()`; a ref is built with `TypeDescription(<ref type>).Types().Get(0)` plus `XMLValue`, fixed in query.py and the fake; (2) the template returned 15 per-subconto rows for account 521.1 with split sums equal to the OData reference values; (3) real publication descriptors write `ib="File=D:\path;"` unquoted, the parity proof parser now accepts it (regression test added). PARITY PROOF (label parity_proof): OData publication `erp_mcp_probe`, whose descriptor ib path is the disposable clone `818HA_negative_write_probe_v2`, versus COM on that same path; 7 accounts, as_of 2026-08-31 23:59:59: 21 rows on each side, 21 matched keys, 0 only-in-either, 0 amount mismatches, identical sha256 of the canonical rows, database file size and mtime unchanged during the run. Limit: the metadata fingerprint was not compared live (both sides carried the clone path identity), so this proves row equality on one database, not fingerprint equality. Private manifest (outside Git): `D:\ERP_MCP_Testbed\real1c_e2e\private_evidence\hybrid_parity\`. No Apache or publication change was needed. The disposable report clone `818HA_report_gen_20261007_172853` (about 4 GB) is no longer needed and must be deleted by the operator (shell policy blocks recursive delete).
Hybrid route stage 6 (governance): scope-freeze rebaseline recorded in SCOPE_FREEZE §2.1, GOVERNANCE §2A, MASTER_PLAN P6, TDD FR-J and traceability row HYB-1; runbook live status updated; engineering checkpoint re-pinned and the dashboard re-synced (document consistency PASS).
- Hybrid route sprint-end local review (three narrow sonnet-medium reviewers, read-only: bridge trust boundary, route/fail-closed handling, test adequacy; no BLOCKER; findings closed in one batch). Closed MAJORs: (1) OData rows without the company property were accepted and the COM route had no row-level company check; now the company property is required on OData rows, the COM template selects `Организация`, the bridge fails closed on a row of another company and the gateway client and normalizer compare `company_ref` again (wire contract and template digest changed; the live parity proof was re-run with the new template: 21 of 21 rows equal, same database); (2) a timed-out COM call could leave the 1C query running while the next request opened a second session, and a slow binding could occupy the shared thread pool; the binding is now poisoned until the abandoned query returns (fail fast `COM_UNAVAILABLE`), at most two requests per binding are admitted; (3) tests did not prove the company wiring, drift on the COM route, provenance per field, the no-fallback factory/binding call counts or the metadata-fingerprint clause of the parity harness; each now has a failing-on-mutation assertion. Closed MINORs: a full page is always reported truncated; amounts are parsed with a strict numeric pattern; success audit is written after the response parts are built; COM client creation is locked; the external company reference is canonicalised before it goes to the bridge; `as_of` limited to 1990-2100 on both sides; body cap on Content-Length; the COM route answers CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED for a mapping other than the bridge's fixed register and company field. Accepted and documented (not code): bridge identity and metadata fingerprint are operator attestation and the bridge does not refuse the reference clone path itself (THREAT_MODEL, runbook); duplicate-row detection and the source-side `has_more` semantics of the real sidecar remain unverified and go to the backlog.

- CORRECTION (2026-10-07, evidence changed): the DAD materials audit and the earlier decision-log entry stated that per-counterparty analytics is unreachable because the published OData exposes only the accounting register entity and its _RecordType entity. That was wrong: only entity sets were checked, not function imports. The live capability profile lists `balance`, `extDimensions`, `recordsWithExtDimensions` as available for `AccountingRegister_Хозрасчетный`, and read-only GETs through the lane proxy as the reader returned rows from `Balance(Period=...)` (Account_Key, ExtDimension1..3 with types, Dr/Cr balances) and `RecordsWithExtDimensions(StartPeriod=...,EndPeriod=...)` (ExtDimensionDr1..3 / Cr1..3). Gap A is therefore a missing semantic tool, not a missing route. Operator decision: hybrid, OData primary and COM fallback for bases where analytics is not published (capability-routed, fixed query templates, parity-checked).

## 2026-10-09 — ChatGPT connector accepts the tunnel resource audience

- Symptom: ChatGPT showed Reconnect for ERP_MCP_REAL1 (connection expired), and a second Reconnect after the first. The tunnel and the public test IdP were healthy.
- Root cause (reproduced with the ChatGPT auth-code flow): ChatGPT tokens carry aud = the OpenAI tunnel resource URL; the gateway accepted only http://127.0.0.1:21000/mcp, so tunnel traffic got 401 invalid_token. The lane env already exported BAG_OAUTH_ADDITIONAL_AUDIENCES but the code ignored it.
- Fix: Settings.oauth_additional_audiences (comma-separated) and oauth_audience_list used by JWTTokenVerifier; envctl registers the tunnel resource for the erp-mcp-chatgpt test client. Test: tests/test_auth.py::test_additional_audience_is_accepted. Test-lane only; no production grant or API change. Supersedes the uncommitted copy of these edits in the integration-candidate worktree.

- 2026-10-09 Machine-profile hardening round 2: closes the findings deferred in the previous entry (F-1, F-5, F-6, F-7, F-8, F-9) from the silent-failure-hunter report. Operator order 2026-10-09: finish everything; the GPT-PM channel was still unavailable, so no exact-head GPT-PM verdict exists (same situation as PR #27); the branch docs/document-registry is reused (no new branch). F-1 (kill switch): new operator command 'scripts/semantic_profiles.py reverify --profile-id --actor --artifacts-root --plans-dir' re-runs the full machine proof (artifact digests and bytes, exact decimals, authorized Rosetta plan status, approver, scope hash) against the stored evidence of a VALIDATED machine profile; a failed proof moves the profile to STALE in the same transaction, writes an audit event (action MAPPING_CHANGED_INVALIDATED with details.reason MACHINE_REVERIFY_FAILED and the failure text, because the event vocabulary has no dedicated action and a new action would need a migration) and the CLI exits 2; a native profile or a non-VALIDATED one is refused. The gateway itself still does not read private artifacts at runtime (they live on the operator host; machine profiles exist in the test environment only and production refuses the allow-list), so the kill switch is operator-run, not automatic: run it before relying on a machine profile and after any plan or artifact change. F-5: company-scope resolution and explain_access now name the real cause (message 'machine-reconciled, not native-validated' / company_operation 'profile_not_native_validated') instead of a stale or missing mapping. F-6: the VALIDATED audit event now carries evidence_basis (NATIVE or MACHINE) and, for machine evidence, authorized_by and evidence_manifest_fingerprint; the profile-list UI badge is NOT done (UI change, backlog). F-7: the profile lookup is deterministic and prefers a native profile over a machine one (ORDER BY machine flag, version, validated_at, profile_id); before, a higher-numbered machine profile could displace a native one. F-8: probe_portfolio raises when source-upsert fails and reports an overall 'passed' flag. F-9: the registry generator warns for every skipped item (missing worktree root, symlink, path escape, uninspectable file) and --check exits 1 when an untracked Markdown path could not be inspected. Tests (my measurements): tests/functional_live/test_machine_hardening_round2.py with 8 PostgreSQL tests (reverify keeps a valid profile; flips STALE on a changed artifact byte; flips STALE when the plan is cancelled; refuses a native profile; machine versus native audit details; F-5 error and explain cause; F-7 native preferred although the machine version is higher; F-8 probe raises), all passing on a disposable PostgreSQL 16 with migrations 001-014; the touched suites (functional_live, machine profile, evidence basis, machine reconciliation, synthetic fixture profiles, SC-08, admin mutations/API/CLI, company scope, semantic profiles) pass: 148 + 86 passed, 2 skipped (pre-existing skips); ruff clean, bandit -q -r src rc 0; registry synthetic driver 28/28. Not executed / unproven: the F-9 warning paths were not exercised by an automated test (no portable way to make a stat call fail on this account), only the unchanged 28 cases were re-run; the reverify command was exercised through the function, not through a spawned CLI process. - Phase 2 S5 test_safe_parser stalled_partial (2026-10-10): root cause is a TEST defect, not a run_isolated defect. On Windows multiprocessing Pipe is message-mode with no length header, so the child's 6-byte write is one complete undecodable message and run_isolated correctly returns CHILD_ABORTED (fail-closed) unless the child starts slower than the 1.0 s deadline; the result therefore flipped with machine load/start-up time. Fix: the partial frame is written only on POSIX, on Windows the child sends nothing so TIMEOUT is deterministic, plus a Windows-only test pinning CHILD_ABORTED for a short message. safe_parser.py unchanged. Full offline tests/phase2: 1986 passed, 229 skipped, 0 failed. S6 report updated after GPT-PM APPROVE (push c194760).

- Phase 2 sprint S8 review fix batch (2026-10-10): four internal read-only reviewers on 080c76d (functional-test-reviewer, silent-failure-hunter, python-reviewer, security-reviewer) found 0 BLOCKER, 2 HIGH and about 10 MAJOR; fixed in ONE batch over four streams. (1) Company isolation: the ledger-backed paths (cards, annotate, annotations, rerun, verify_original, enqueue/rerun params) now take an OwnershipPort and refuse NOT_IN_SCOPE before any read with an identical shape for foreign and unknown ids; the viewer scope is no longer self-asserted: every facade method takes the acting session and checks EntitlementPort first (non-member and unknown company are indistinguishable, the epoch is not probeable before entitlement); the epoch authority is required everywhere and is a callable re-read before disclosure/commit. (2) Rerun atomicity: workbench_review splits check_rerun (pure) from commit_rerun; the idempotency store reserves a PENDING slot, runs the action outside the global lock, and records DONE/EFFECT_DONE so a failed dispatch retried with the same key completes the same run and never answers STALE. (3) Quotas are per tenant and per actor under a global ceiling, with observable overflow, bounded effect/dispatcher logs and heap-based expiry; review log caps per run/tenant. (4) Boundary: operation is derived from the job kind (mismatch is OPERATION_DENIED), capture comes from an injected CapturePolicyPort, keyed_csrf_derive exists, dispatcher output is validated, orphan jobs are cancelled, new codes DEPENDENCY_FAILED and ORIGINAL_UNVERIFIABLE. (5) Views: attestations only when bound to the viewer's own runs, exact (not NFKC/casefold) tenant and company match before any read, foreign junk cannot change or break the viewer's output, a refused matrix shows a fixed code, view digests are recomputed on construction, events GAP/SOURCE_UNAVAILABLE/REVOKED downgrade LIVE_CURRENT. (6) comparison= cards no longer certify caller numbers (ORIGINAL_UNVERIFIABLE unless a totals row equals the ledger). Accepted residuals: card_digest is unkeyed self-consistency; epoch re-check is read-then-commit not atomic; annotate has no idempotency key and no jobs_api decision function; EFFECT_DONE entries expire with normal retention; fakes (correlation ids, job ids) are sequential and guessable; a revoked attestation still shows REVOKED at an earlier injected clock (kept by design and by an existing test); coverage matrix rows match by normalized scope_key; INCONCLUSIVE card state is not tested at facade level; one POISON_FIELDS early return in test_jobs_api.py remains. Process: streams G1/G2 wrote code before most tests (red-then-green only partly observed), G3 wrote tests first (29 of 36 red), G4 wrote the facade before tests; nothing mutation-tested (operator ban). tests/phase2 offline 4433 passed, 229 skipped; ruff clean. - Phase 2 S7/S8 GPT-PM round-2 remediation (2026-10-10): the bounded verification of head 2d4bb5b returned REJECT with 16 of 21 findings FIXED and 5 NOT_FIXED; all five were checked against code and fixed in one batch. S7 M03: the direct-root branch of the membership walk now honours removed roots (a removed root no longer authorizes its own id; only an explicit feed UPSERT re-qualification with fresh metadata reinstates it). S7 M05: the persisted snapshot marker is now bound to a per-tracker generation id (ResnapshotTracker.persistent_marker/token_from_marker); a marker from another generation is never continued and restarts from a new snapshot, so a restarted tracker's newer requirement cannot be cleared by colliding order numbers. S8 M05: the rerun registers a pending-audit marker right after the run exists, retries the audit append once, and a later commit_rerun of the same plan adopts that run and finishes its audit instead of creating a second one (the unaudited run stays visible in the ledger until recovery; the ledger has no delete API - accepted residual). S8 M06: an idempotent mutation now requires a dispatcher that supports find plus dispatch_key; a legacy 4-argument dispatcher is refused with DEPENDENCY_FAILED before any side effect (test dispatchers were updated to the 5-argument contract). S8 M08: scope fields of a returned job record are read defensively; a forged record with unset slots is indistinguishable from a missing job. Tests: tests/phase2/test_s7_s8_gpt_fix_round2.py (9 tests); tests/phase2 offline 4571 passed, 229 skipped; ruff clean. - Phase 2 S7/S8 GPT-PM round-3 remediation (2026-10-10): the round-2 verification of head 5c88065 returned REJECT (S8-M08 FIXED; S7-M03, S7-M05, S8-M05, S8-M06 each had a verified residual or regression). All four fixed in one batch. S7-M03: removed-root re-qualification is now PROVISIONAL: prepare_page keeps it in a task-local ContextVar (a concurrent disclosure never sees it), nothing is discarded from the removed set during prepare, and the root becomes live again only through the new MembershipChecker.accept_page(preparation) after the cursor commit; a refused, replaced or unaccepted page never reinstates and prepare_page's finally block drops every provisional cache entry. S7-M05: tracker generation is a 128-bit random id, unique inside the process by construction (an issued-set skips repeats), marker = generation<<32|order (cursor snap range widened); cross-process collision probability 2**-128, no durable counter exists (accepted residual). S8-M05: the pending-audit marker now stores (run, key, snapshot, digest, actor); recovery re-runs tenant, epoch and ownership gates, verifies the pending run is that request's result and the same actor, and the audit append is idempotent per created run (an append that wrote then raised cannot produce a second audit row). S8-M06: _require_fenced_dispatcher refuses a dispatcher without find plus dispatch_key support before the idempotency reservation and before the gate's effect step in BOTH decide_enqueue and decide_rerun. Tests: test_s7_s8_gpt_fix_round2.py now 17 tests; tests/phase2 offline 4579 passed, 229 skipped; ruff clean. NOT_RUN: PostgreSQL, CI, real Google, real UI, Release 1 regression, mutation runs. S7-M03: the ambient ContextVar was inherited by child asyncio tasks; it is gone. The provisional root set is now an EXPLICIT argument threaded through _check/_resolve/_resolve_shortcut/_walk by prepare_page only, and a result computed under it is never cached. Durable reinstatement requires accept_page(preparation, committed_cursor): one-shot, same preparation object, same scope epoch (advance_epoch clears the pending preparation) and a receipt equal to the batch's committable cursor. S8-M05: check_rerun/_plan now treats a previous run that is no longer the chain head as recoverable only when the review log holds a pending-audit record for the same key and snapshot and the current head is exactly that pending run superseding the previous one; the public WorkbenchSession.rerun retry therefore reaches commit_rerun's recovery branch (actor, digest, ownership, epoch re-verified there) and yields one run, one audit entry, one job and a replayable ticket; a different snapshot stays RERUN_TARGET_STALE. Tests: test_s7_s8_gpt_fix_round2.py now 22 tests; tests/phase2 offline 4584 passed, 229 skipped; ruff clean. Accepted residuals unchanged plus: cross-process generation collision 2**-128; unaudited in-memory run visible in the ledger until recovery (no ledger delete API). NOT_RUN: PostgreSQL, CI, real Google, real UI, Release 1 regression, mutation runs. Next: GPT-PM verification of these two on the new exact head; no push before APPROVE.
- Phase 2 S7/S8 GPT-PM round-5 remediation (2026-10-10): round 4 (head a277d81) returned REJECT with three verified scenarios. S7-M03: accept_page no longer trusts a caller-computed cursor string; DriveCursorStore.commit now issues a CursorCommitReceipt (nonce remembered in a bounded per-store table; token, cursor version, epoch) only after a successful durable commit, and accept_page(preparation, receipt) requires a type-exact receipt that the issuing store still recognises, token equal to the batch's committable cursor, epoch equal to the checker's, the same unreplaced preparation and an unchanged scope epoch. A forged, lookalike-store, foreign-token or computed value stays denied. S8-M05 A: recovery of an already written audit entry (ack lost after the write) no longer needs a quota slot: _plan skips the new-entry room check when the pending run's audit entry exists, and commit_rerun reconciles it (ReviewLog._audit_exists) before reserving. S8-M05 B: the recovery path rechecks the live epoch immediately before any audit effect or reconciliation (fixed SCOPE_EPOCH_STALE, no append, no dispatch). Tests: round2 file now 24 tests + helper tests/phase2/s7_receipts.py (genuine receipts through a real fake-backed store); tests/phase2 offline 4586 passed, 229 skipped; ruff clean. NOT_RUN: PostgreSQL, CI, real Google, real UI, Release 1 regression, mutation runs. Next: GPT-PM verification of these on the new exact head; no push before APPROVE.
- Phase 2 S7/S8 GPT-PM round-6 remediation (2026-10-10): round 5 (head e6778fa) closed S8-M05 A and B and REJECTed only the S7-M03 receipt trust boundary plus its direct regression R5-R01. The receipt is now an opaque handle with a private nonce and no self-reported fields; what it proves lives in the issuing DriveCursorStore registry (nonce -> namespace, tenant, connection, PRIOR cursor, committed cursor, version, epoch; bounded). MembershipChecker takes a trusted commit_store at construction (none bound = nothing is ever reinstated) and accept_page asks store.receipt_matches(receipt, identity, prior cursor the page was prepared against, cursor the batch may commit, epoch), which uses exact-type checks on every input (hostile str/int/identity subclasses, bool epochs, half-initialized or foreign receipts return False, never raise). A receipt from another store, connection, prior cursor or committed cursor is not proof. Accepted residual: Python has no true privacy, so in-process code that writes the bound store's private _issued table can forge an acknowledgment (same class as the accepted AST-boundary residual); the real boundary is the production cursor store, which is outside this offline module. Tests: round2 file now 26 tests (+ helper tests/phase2/s7_receipts.py); tests/phase2 offline 4588 passed, 229 skipped; ruff clean. NOT_RUN: PostgreSQL, CI, real Google, real UI, Release 1 regression, mutation runs. Next: GPT-PM verification of S7-M03 only on the new exact head; no push before APPROVE.
- Phase 2 S7 GPT-PM round-7 remediation (2026-10-10): round 6 (head c125c3a) closed R5-R01 and left S7-M03 open with three genuine-receipt scenarios (wrong corpus on the bound store, same tokens with different effects, obsolete version). The store registry entry now also binds the corpus fingerprint, the cursor version and a digest of the committed events (events_digest over the canonical event list); DriveCursorStore tracks the newest version it committed per cursor (_latest) so an older receipt is obsolete after any later commit. accept_page recomputes the expected corpus fingerprint from the checker's corpus and the expected events digest from the preparation's own batch via the canonical page_events converter (renamed from _page_events; the same function the baseline uses to commit a page), then asks store.receipt_matches(receipt, identity, corpus fingerprint, prior cursor, committed cursor, epoch, events digest). A same-store receipt for another corpus, a commit with the same tokens but empty or different events, and an obsolete receipt are all refused; a genuine commit of exactly the prepared page is accepted. Tests: round2 file now 29 tests, helper tests/phase2/s7_receipts.py rebuilt (corpus-aware, page-event receipts); tests/phase2 offline 4591 passed, 229 skipped; ruff clean. Accepted residuals unchanged (Python private registry write; an honest caller can still lie about events only by writing the store's private state). NOT_RUN: PostgreSQL, CI, real Google, real UI, Release 1 regression, mutation runs. Next: GPT-PM verification of S7-M03 on the new exact head; no push before APPROVE.
- Phase 2 S7 GPT-PM round-8 remediation (2026-10-10): round 7 (head 1e002de) closed the wrong-corpus scenario and found two MAJOR: R7-M01 (events digest computed from the caller's mutable list after the awaited commit) and the obsolete-receipt fence (a store-local version cache is not authoritative across writers or reordered replies). Fixes: DriveCursorStore.commit takes a canonical immutable snapshot of the events (jsonb_text + json.loads) BEFORE the first await, submits exactly that snapshot to the port and digests exactly that text, so later caller mutation cannot change what a receipt proves; the _latest version cache is deleted. Freshness is now authoritative: the registry keeps the commit's lease and the exact committed record value, and the new async DriveCursorStore.receipt_is_current re-reads the durable cursor through the port and requires the same version AND the same value (so another writer, another store instance on the same backend, or a reordered older reply all make the receipt obsolete). MembershipChecker.accept_page is therefore async; it no longer burns the preparation on a failed attempt, and after the backend read it re-checks that the same preparation is still pending at an unchanged epoch before applying (two concurrent acceptances apply exactly once). Tests: round2 file now 34 tests (events mutated mid-commit, second store on the same backend, delayed reply of an older commit, concurrent double accept); tests/phase2 offline 4595 passed, 229 skipped; ruff clean. Accepted residuals unchanged. NOT_RUN: PostgreSQL, CI, real Google, real UI, Release 1 regression, mutation runs. Next: GPT-PM verification of these two on the new exact head; no push before APPROVE.
- Phase 2 S7 round-9 DESIGN DECISION (2026-10-10): the S7-M03 re-qualification path was removed instead of patched a ninth time. Rounds 2-8 each hardened the optional mechanism that re-admits a feed-removed Drive root (provisional set, ContextVar, accept_page, cursor-commit receipts, registry bindings, authoritative re-read) and every round exposed another read-then-act window that no offline in-memory module can close atomically (round 8 asks for a backend-enforced version-bound claim, which only a real durable store can provide). Per the operator contract (NO SECONDARY BLOCKERS: a safeguard that keeps generating review rounds is disabled by default instead of patched again) the reinstatement mechanism is deleted: a root the change feed reported removed STAYS removed in this checker (fail closed); its own id and every descendant are refused, a feed UPSERT never revives it, and it returns only when a NEW MembershipChecker is built after a re-consent / resnapshot (control test). A removed root that is also reachable under another still-live declared root stays reachable through that root. Deleted: MembershipChecker.accept_page/commit_store/_pending_reinstate/provisional plumbing, CursorCommitReceipt/receipt registry/events_digest/receipt_matches/receipt_is_current/StoreCommit.receipt, the immutable-event snapshot that existed only for the receipt, tests/phase2/s7_receipts.py and every receipt test (R7-M01, R8-M01 and the freshness TOCTOU are therefore moot by construction). Kept: round-2..4 fixes (direct-root branch honours removed roots), the 128-bit tracker generation and widened snap range, all S8 fixes. Tests: round2 file now 17 tests incl. upsert-never-revives, refused page, concurrent disclosure, new-checker control, nested live root; tests/phase2 offline 4584 passed, 229 skipped; ruff clean. NOT_RUN: PostgreSQL, CI, real Google, real UI, Release 1 regression, mutation runs. Operator note: if automatic re-admission of a removed root is ever required, it belongs to the production cursor store with a backend-enforced claim, as a separate scoped task. Next: GPT-PM verification of S7-M03 on the new exact head; no push before APPROVE.

- Phase 2 S9 GPT-PM round 1 remediation (2026-10-10): GPT-PM rejected head 292a62d with 0 BLOCKER and 9 MAJOR, all fixed in one batch. M01 plan_budget refuses (BACKEND_BUDGET_EXCEEDED) when the shared total cannot hold the guaranteed background minimum plus one interactive slot per interactive backend; two older K2 tests were adapted to that rule. M02 writer accounting is keyed by (tenant, source). M03 audit overflow is counted per (tenant, company). M04 quarantine entries carry the company and quarantine_count is company-scoped. M05 DeliverySinkPort.publish takes company_id and the fake sink dedups on (tenant, company, source, connection, event_id). M06 release_rollback snapshots every step once (_snapshot_step) and uses that snapshot for classification, grants and the digest. M07 retention objects are keyed by company+kind+id and decisions evaluate that company's holds plus tenant-level holds. M08 restore verification fails ATTESTATION when an accepted head rests on a revision with no attestation row (the old test that asserted an empty table passes was the defect and was rewritten). M09 ReadinessReport and SlotResult validate their invariants in __post_init__ so a fabricated PASSED or non-EVALUATION_ONLY authority cannot be constructed. - Phase 2 S9 GPT-PM round 2 remediation (2026-10-10): at 23a055f GPT-PM confirmed M02-M09 fixed and kept only S9-M01 open: the shared-total preflight did not stop phase B from giving interactive work all of a backend's capacity when writer, capture or tenant-share caps had deferred the background minimum. Decision: the guaranteed background minimum is a RESERVATION of physical capacity, not a grant. plan_budget computes per backend the unmet part of min(min_background_slots, demand) after phase A; interactive work on that backend may use at most per_backend_limit minus used minus unmet, and the unmet total is also held back from the shared total. BudgetPlan gains background_reserved (included in the digest) so the unused reservation is explicit. Considered and rejected: refusing policies where the minimum exceeds tenant_share_slots or capture_per_backend, because several tenants or writer sources can legitimately reach the minimum together and the stricter check broke valid plans (three existing tests). The preflight refusal stays when the shared total cannot hold the interactive backends plus all reservations. New tests cover the GPT-PM B1/B2 writer case, tenant-share-constrained, capture-constrained, feasible and total-too-small cases. Tests: tests/phase2 6122 passed, 229 skipped, ruff clean; not seen red (no mutation runs). Status ceiling IMPLEMENTED_UNVERIFIED.
- Phase 2 S9 GPT-PM round 3 remediation (2026-10-10): at 92cc3d9 GPT-PM accepted the reservation semantics for S9-M01 but found phase C of plan_budget still granting extra background work on one backend out of capacity that another backend's outstanding reservation needed, so a returned plan could promise granted + background_reserved above total_limit (two reproductions: capture-limited B1 plus background B2, and a writer spanning B1/B2). Fix: phase C now passes the sum of the OTHER backends' unmet reservations as the shared-total reserve, extra background on a backend fills its own reservation first (unmet is reduced by what phase C grants there), and background_reserved is computed after phase C. The invariant granted + reserved <= total_limit (and per backend granted <= per_backend_limit) is asserted over a deterministic 8-axis grid (over 500 returned plans) plus the two GPT-PM reproductions as tests. The semantics are unchanged; no policy-level refusal was added (GPT-PM agreed). This is the third round on one mechanism, so the planner stays a scripted offline reference and no further hardening is planned beyond what a review proves broken. Tests: tests/phase2 6126 passed, 229 skipped, ruff clean; not seen red (no mutation runs). Status ceiling IMPLEMENTED_UNVERIFIED.

- Semantic accounting tools on the real source (operator GO 2026-10-07, branch `feature/semantic-accounting-real1c` from main c148a57; the parallel hybrid-route session keeps its own worktree). Phase 0 forensics, evidence from the gateway log on `onec-818ha-reference` / 818 HA SRL: `payable_balance`, `accounting_balance_and_turnovers` and `accounting_posting_rows` all raised `SemanticProfileUnavailable` (no validated `payable.balance` / `account.balance_and_turnovers` / `accounting.posting_rows` profile for this exact source/company), and `payable_aging` raised `ValueError: as_of must include an explicit timezone`, then `SemanticProfileUnavailable` (`payable.open_items`) once a timezone was supplied. These are designed fail-closed refusals, not a routing or OData defect; the client saw only `Error executing tool <name>` because the SDK hides the text of any non-ToolError exception. Phase 1 (this change): `tool_errors.StructuredErrorMCPServer` maps a crash/refusal to a bounded JSON payload (code, category, tool, source_id, operation, retryable, safe_message, correlation_id); only classes with author-written text may speak and that text is redacted; PermissionError, transport, audit and unknown errors never echo their message. The error type stays an `UnexpectedToolError` subclass so existing callers and tests are unchanged. Not claimed: the missing validated profiles are NOT created or faked here; a profile is validated only with native 1C reconciliation evidence, which the owner still owes, so Phase 2/3 values from the real source need an explicit operator decision on how an unvalidated-but-independently-reconciled result may be labelled.
- Provisional profiles (operator decision 2026-10-07, option 1): a REAL source that is not yet natively validated may serve semantic tools from a reviewed, sha256-pinned profile file, labelled `profile_kind`/`validation_status` `UNVALIDATED_INDEPENDENT_RECONCILIATION`, `evidence_level` `L2_PROVISIONAL`, `native_reconciliation` `NOT_RUN`, with the reconciliation basis echoed in every response and a `UNVALIDATED_INDEPENDENT_RECONCILIATION_NOT_NATIVE_VALIDATED` warning. Guards: test environment only (settings, constructor, every lookup, production registry refuses a provider); source must carry the `real-reference` tag; any live DB profile for the same source/company/concept shadows it; metadata drift is reported as `SCHEMA_DRIFT`, not flattened; it never becomes a `bag.semantic_profiles` row and never counts towards the native-report rule. Implementation: `ProvisionalProfiles` (subclass of the fixture provider), `Registry._fallback_mapping`/`_pinned_mapping`, settings `BAG_PROVISIONAL_PROFILES_FILE`/`_SHA256`. Tests: tests/test_provisional_profiles.py (17), tests/test_tool_errors.py (26); full unit suite 1640 passed, 182 skipped. - Phase 2 G4 probe on the real Google Drive (2026-10-10, operator consent given in the browser, scope drive.file only): login OK, refresh token stored in D:/secure/erp_mcp (outside git). Observed facts: (1) the pre-existing operator folder 18n8vf... is NOT visible: files.get 404, children list 0, files visible to the app 0; (2) the app can create its own folder and a file in it (both HTTP 200); (3) a file the operator uploaded by hand into the app-created folder is NOT visible to the app (children list returns only the app-created file). Conclusion: with drive.file the app sees only files it created itself, so reading files that people drop into a folder (the capture use case of S7) needs a broader read scope (drive.readonly) or an app-owned upload flow. This is a measured result of one probe on one account, not a G4 PASS: G4 stays NOT PASSED until the operator decides the scope (drive.readonly = read access to the whole Drive, read-only) and the polling/changes behaviour is tested against it. Test artifacts left in the operator Drive: folder ERP_MCP_G4_PROBE (1Q4nFrS3eKHNTqJU71hrJ6CN_-zJpVvjS) with app_created_probe.txt and the operator-uploaded file; deletion is the operator decision. The probe script is in the session scratchpad only, not in git.
- Phase 2 G4 probe with drive.readonly (2026-10-10, operator consent in the browser; operator chose option A): login OK, separate refresh token stored in D:/secure/erp_mcp/google_drive_token_readonly.json (outside git). Observed: the operator folder 18n8vf... is now readable (files.get 200, a folder) but its children list is 0 (the folder is empty or holds nothing visible); the app can list at least 20 files of the whole Drive (page size limit), which confirms that this scope is read access to the entire Drive. Nothing was created or changed in this probe. Open: whether a file the operator drops into that folder appears in the children list and in the changes feed, and the decision whether a read scope over the whole Drive is acceptable for production; G4 stays NOT PASSED. Scope trade-off recorded: drive.file sees only app-created files, drive.readonly sees every file read-only.
- Phase 2 G4 changes-feed probe (2026-10-10, operator asked me to create the file and verify myself): a start page token was taken with the drive.readonly token, then a new text file was created in the app-created folder ERP_MCP_G4_PROBE with the drive.file token; the changes feed read with the drive.readonly token returned that file (right name, right parent folder) on the 2nd poll, 3 seconds apart, and the folder listing showed it next to app_created_probe.txt and the operator file user_added_probe. Measured facts on one account: (1) polling through the changes feed works for new files with drive.readonly without a webhook; (2) the same files are invisible to drive.file when created by someone else. Not proven: behaviour for moves, renames, shared-drive items, revoked access and token expiry (the app is in Testing mode, refresh tokens may expire after 7 days), and the scope decision for production. - Remote 1C host (Ivan) test environment and MCP stack (2026-10-11): at the operator's request the 1C platform and a copy of the lane test base were installed on the second machine over key-only SSH through Tailscale, and a full copy of the Release 1 gateway stack (portable PostgreSQL 16, Redis, Apache OData publication, lane proxies, sidecar, test IdP, gateway) was brought up there and kept alive by an idempotent scheduled task. Evidence: OData metadata under the read-only reader returned HTTP 200 with the same byte size as the local lane; MCP tools were called over the Tailscale address with a host-minted token and nine data tools succeeded, with payable_balance and the other register-backed tools unsupported because the base metadata has no matching register. Corrections: the 2026-10-10 canary claim that accounting_balance_by_analytics failed for lack of a profile was wrong (the call lacked a timezone offset in as_of; with an offset it returns the machine-reconciled supplier summary); the G6 canary document was corrected. Operator decisions: the operator authorised a test-lane profile mode in the Release 1 worktree (separate local commit ea4defa in that worktree, not pushed) so every test-lane read works with honest NOT_RECONCILED labels; it is test-environment only and never counts as native or machine evidence. Operator-only and open: the OpenAI tunnel id and runtime API key for the ChatGPT connector, enabling Tailscale HTTPS/Funnel or a named Cloudflare tunnel for a stable public issuer. Scripts are under scripts/remote; the runbook is docs/phase2/artifacts/REMOTE_1C_HOST_STACK_2026-10-11.md. Staging was not used and no production action was taken.
- Remote host ChatGPT path (2026-10-11): at the operator's request an OpenAI tunnel client was connected on the 1C host and the test IdP was published through Tailscale Funnel so the issuer is stable. The operator pasted a runtime API key into a PowerShell prompt and thereby exposed it in a screenshot; the key was declared compromised and the operator supplied a replacement through a local file that was piped over SSH stdin into a DPAPI blob on the host (never printed, never on a command line). Evidence: doctor ok, tunnel-client started and /readyz 200, public OpenID discovery returns the Funnel issuer, all nine supported MCP data tools still answer after the issuer switch. Open: the ChatGPT connector login was not observed; the old key must be revoked by the operator; the key source file must be deleted by the operator.
- ChatGPT connector creation blocker on the remote host (2026-10-11): the operator's first attempt to add the custom MCP server was rejected with 'Some app settings were rejected'. Evidence: the tunnel-client log on the host showed 'unsupported channel harpoon' for commands polled from ChatGPT and 'base URL must use https' for the harpoon targets, because the MCP target on the Tailscale address is plain http. Decision: run tunnel-client with --harpoon.allow-plaintext-http (scripts/remote/run-tunnel.ps1); after the restart the log shows harpoon target_count=2 with no registration failure. Not verified: whether the ChatGPT form now accepts the connector; the retry is operator-interactive. The public IdP host is skipped by harpoon registration as public, which is expected.
- ChatGPT connector created on the remote host (2026-10-11): after the test IdP was moved from the Tailscale Funnel port 8443 to the standard port 443 (issuer https://ivan.tail08f9f9.ts.net/realms/erp-mcp-test, no port), the operator reported that the custom MCP server (plugin) was created; the earlier rejection 'Some app settings were rejected' occurred with the :8443 issuer and the harpoon plaintext flag alone did not clear it, so the non-standard issuer port is the most likely cause (the operator's report is the only evidence; ChatGPT's own error body was not seen). Observed: tunnel-client forwards MCP commands on the main channel after the creation; the OAuth login and a tool call from ChatGPT were not yet observed. Claude token was re-issued for the new issuer. Open: ChatGPT sign-in and first tool call, deletion of the connector parameter sheet by the operator, revocation of the exposed old key.

- Phase 2 test-lane MCP server, stage 1 (2026-10-11): at the operator request a small read-only MCP server was added in the new top-level directory testlane/phase2_mcp/ (outside src/business_ai_gateway/phase2, so the offline package keeps its no-network and no-Release-1-import invariants; its boundary tests stay green). It reads two Release 1 gateways (base A local, base B host Ivan, copies of the same base) and feeds R2 reconciliation.compare_statements with per-account six-column data (gateway rows summed per account, account as analytic key, totals derived from rows, side A in the native slot only positionally). Measured live: the turnover tool returns at most 200 sparse rows with has_more=true and no paging argument, so R2 correctly returns INCONCLUSIVE; the 521.1 closing balances are compared by a plain exact-decimal PARTIAL comparison (own states PARTIAL_MATCH/PARTIAL_MISMATCH) and were equal on both bases; purchase_documents lacks contract_ref and deletion_mark and spans counterparties, so compare_posted_purchases is NOT_SUPPORTED and only a first-page digest is reported. A SYNTHETIC_NEGATIVE_CONTROL perturbs one side-B row in memory and shows R2 finds the exact injected difference. Honesty rules: MATCH is never native PASS, incomplete data is INCONCLUSIVE, amounts and names only with show_values=true, tokens and values never logged. Not covered (stage 2): S8 job API endpoints, living.* SQL ports, Drive connector, inbound OAuth. Design: docs/phase2/artifacts/PHASE2_TESTLANE_MCP_DESIGN.md. Tests: tests/testlane (19 offline tests). Status stays NO-GO / IMPLEMENTED_UNVERIFIED / EVALUATION_ONLY.

- Real-difference two-base reconciliation, stage 1 verified live (2026-10-11): at the operator request ("test the whole Phase 2 functionality, change something, then reconcile the reference machine against the other machine") a disposable local clone of the test base was created (818HA_p2diff, byte-identical to 818HA_test_ready by SHA-256 before the edit), ONE posted purchase document of 2026-08 was edited through the 1C COM connector using the clone administrator login (DPAPI-protected, from the existing secrets directory; never printed) and reposted (+100.00 on the service line; accounting movement Dt 713.8 / Kt 521.1 became 2600 instead of 2500), the changed 1CD file was copied to the host Ivan (SHA-256 verified equal on both ends) and the host OData alias erp_mcp_ref was repointed to it (revert: scripts/remote/repoint-odata-base.ps1 -BaseName 818HA_test_ready). Evidence: before the edit the two sides were identical (baseline digests equal); after it the Phase 2 test-lane MCP tool phase2_reconcile_two_bases returned supplier_521_1_closing_partial = PARTIAL_MISMATCH / CLOSING_VALUES_DIFFER with exactly one difference (ROW_MISSING_IN_A, a credit-100 row for the edited counterparty present only on side B), confirmed directly through both gateways (A: no row for that counterparty, B: balance_credit 100). The six-column per-account section stays INCONCLUSIVE (the turnover tool has no paging argument, so a page with has_more=true can never be complete) and the purchase-documents section stays NOT_SUPPORTED (no contract_ref/deletion_mark in the listing); both are correct refusals, not failures. Limits: both sides carry machine-reconciled labels, so this is a copy-consistency detection and NOT a native 1C PASS or independent-origin evidence; show_values=true adds analytic references but not the amounts of the differing row (candidate improvement for stage 2). Host has no 1C client license, so edits to a copy can only be made on the licensed local machine. New files: testlane/tools/edit_purchase_on_clone.py (refuses any base path except the p2diff clone, dry run by default), scripts/remote/repoint-odata-base.ps1, scripts/remote/store-onec-admin.ps1 (stores the clone administrator login as a DPAPI blob on the host; the host copy cannot connect without a client license, the local copy is proved by one COM connect). Release 2 remains NO-GO; G1/G3/G4/G5/G6 NOT PASSED.
