- v2 controlled-change closure plan written and approved at plan level (2026-10-11): operator decisions of the session. (1) Scope: "read-only was Phase 1"; version 2 must change 1C data by prompt from ChatGPT or Claude (for example "add this invoice", "update this contract") and be able to reconcile the result. The recorded Phase 2 scope had no writes (R2-REQ-15 "no business writes"), so the plan adds a new program S11-S19 and a new gate G8 instead of reading the old story catalogue as complete. (2) The operator objected to unrequested subagent launches while only a plan was asked for: agents are launched only with explicit permission for that run; the default review channel is GPT-PM. Deliverables: docs/phase2/PLAN_V2_CONTROLLED_CHANGE_2026-10-11.md (English durable record, generated from one bilingual data set), reports/PHASE2_V2_CLOSURE_PLAN_2026-10-11.ru.html and .html. GPT-PM review (PM Bridge project erp_mcp_r2, a new chat registered for this repository on 2026-10-11 because it had no binding): revision 2 REJECT (8 BLOCKER, 12 MAJOR, 8 MINOR/INFO), revision 3 REJECT (15 findings closed, 3 MAJOR: idempotency identity and create locking, per-operation grant accounting, single writer versus target-local parking), revision 4 REJECT (3 closed, 1 MAJOR regression: forced termination of a 1C session conditional in one place and unconditional in three), revision 5 APPROVE, sha256 303974518dd7dfdcdff7b281450abfecbfc0552be2892bdf7c62ce0dede497fe, as the planning baseline for S11-S19. Authorized by that verdict: S11 documentation and execution planning only (contracts CT1-CT15 as decision records, threat model, source-evidence inventory, CT14 feasibility specification). NOT authorized: the S12 interface freeze (needs a separate GPT-PM review of the completed S11 threat model and the authorization and recovery contracts), any code, push, deployment, production write; G8 NOT_PASSED, C1 NOT_REACHED, Release 2 NO-GO, G0-G7 and G6-pre unchanged. Decisions D1-D3 approved by GPT-PM (draft by default and posting as a separate approved change; v2.0 catalogue = purchase document creation, allow-listed contract update, post and unpost, with no payments, deletion, sales or bulk operations; the write service stays private and is never published on the public tunnel by default). Key design contracts added by the review rounds: authenticated principal and person identity with a non-bearer approval record (CT1); a durable intent plus a logical request key with an immutable payload digest, an atomic marker inside the 1C write, create locking by canonical business key, and APPLY_UNKNOWN resolved only by a trusted complete observation (CT2); no claim of a fence inside 1C, per-target locks, compare-and-write and call ownership with PROVEN_FINISHED versus POSSIBLY_EXECUTING (CT3); target classes by authenticated base fingerprint with staging always denied (CT4); signed external audit checkpoints and SAFE_HOLD (CT5); a per-operation budget accounting matrix (CT13); a binary feasibility gate that blocks unsupported mandatory capabilities (CT14). Mutation tests: the review asked for them and the operator ban of 2026-10-09 stands, so they are NOT adopted; GPT-PM accepted the replacement (written assertion justification, data-level negative controls, structural import separation of the verifier, hand-computed fixtures) at plan level. Residual risk recorded for the operator: two logically independent components can share one wrong accounting assumption, and an assertion that looks discriminating may miss an implementation bypass; this is not measured mutation coverage and must stay visible in the G8 evidence and the operator decision record (plan section 17.3, S19). Limits: the reviewer had no repository access, so every statement in the plan about existing R2 modules (aliases, workbench_review, jobs_api, reconciliation, audit_gate, capture_permit, side_effect_boundary, no kill switch in src) stays UNKNOWN to it until the S11 source-evidence inventory; the consistency check of identifiers (47 scenario ids, 46 active, 15 invariants, all references resolve, mappings mirror each other) is my own script, not an independent review. The status report was also updated with the scope-gap note (the recorded scope contained no writes). No test, CI, mutation run or subagent was started. Nothing was pushed: local main is 10 commits ahead of origin/main (8f50ea8) before this commit; the push needs its own GPT-PM APPROVE on the exact head.

- CT14 feasibility spike on the disposable clone (2026-10-12): under the operator's explicit GO a clone-only script copied one posted purchase receipt to a new draft with a marker, read it back, posted it, read the accounting movements by registrar (0 -> 2), unposted it (2 -> 0) and observed the data version change on each write; an exclusive data lock held by one session was refused for a second session on the file base. Measured SUPPORTED for COM on the clone: create-with-marker, read-back, post, movements read (accounting register only), unpost, data version, cross-session lock, session listing. INCONCLUSIVE and not attempted: forced session termination, concurrent writes, payment side effects, narrow rights, OData writes, version-conditioned write, client-server lock behaviour. The marker lives in a user-editable comment field, so it is a hint to be verified by reconciliation, not proof. One unposted draft (marker CT14-3b8ed340807f) remains on the clone; nothing was deleted. Staging and production were not touched. Record: docs/phase2/CT14_FEASIBILITY_SPIKE_2026-10-12.md. No test, CI or mutation run and no subagent was started. Nothing is pushed.

- S11-R01a remediation (2026-10-12): GPT-PM round 3 found that the gate validator trusted the manifest's own graph (MAJOR). The canonical mandatory gates, nodes, predecessor sets and the G7 conditional G4 edge are now hard-coded in the validator for manifest schema 2; a missing or unexpected gate or node, differing predecessors, or another schema number is rejected; a graph amendment needs a reviewed change of the validator constant with a new schema number. A G4 waiver now needs approved_by (GPT-PM or operator), an approval_ref and evidence that quotes the reference with an exact-token marker (APPROVE or OPERATOR_APPROVED); this is an audit-trail check, not a cryptographic proof, and the limit is stated. The e2e-runner agent extended the fixtures: 83 of 83 asserted fixtures behave as expected, two report-only probes, no accepted-but-should-reject case; the runner found two marker-substring weaknesses (OPERATOR_APPROVED and DISAPPROVED satisfying APPROVE) that were fixed. GPT-PM also ruled that S11 cannot close with the create/post/unpost slice included unless D5 (configuration extension), a least-privilege role and a durable marker are settled and qualified on the clone, and that an unmeasured capability is INCONCLUSIVE or BLOCKED_BY_DECISION, not UNSUPPORTED. Those items are operator-gated. No other test, CI or mutation run; nothing pushed.

- S13 remediation batch B round 1 (B1-B3) as a NON-GATING PROTOTYPE (2026-10-14): migration db/phase2/008_change_hardening_b.sql. B1: validate drops the caller-supplied budget flags (3-argument form); new declare_budget stores the grant, currency, amount and counts in change_ledger.change_budget before approval and the approval digest binds it; claim derives grant, amount, counts and key hash from the stored declaration (cross-check arguments only), refuses a missing RESERVED row, a currency mismatch, a draft without business key and any state other than NOT_STARTED/NOT_VERIFIED/not DISPATCHED; records.reservation_state follows the reservation row. B2: budget_admin has assert_scope, same-tenant-and-company updates only, appends BUDGET_GRANT_SET events; new budget_revoke appends BUDGET_GRANT_REVOKED; person_of, same_person, epoch_value, registry_enforced and the new internals are closed to runtime roles (instead of assert_scope). B3: registry defaults to ENFORCED; claim and issue_permit refuse a non-clone target when the registry is not enforced; propose records session_user and assert_session_independent blocks self-approval through SET ROLE or person aliases. 14 new focused tests in tests/phase2/test_change_hardening_b_sql.py; legacy fixtures were updated deliberately (every actor is a registered principal, a default grant exists, propose declares key, class and budget; validate takes 3 arguments) without weakening assertions; port SPEC updated. Agent-reported pytest results on its own disposable container, one file at a time: hardening_b 14, port 16, sweeps 26, registry 62, ledger 69 passed; not independently re-run yet. NOT done: two-person or fixed-list control for registry changes (B3 clause); the operator/witness independence check in operator_resolve and the cosign path still uses same_person without a session check; key-lock exclusion between distinct changes now depends on business-key collision at validate. B4-B6 follow. Prototype results are not gate evidence.

- S13 batch C: B3 open items as a NON-GATING PROTOTYPE (2026-10-14): migration 010_registry_session_hardening.sql. (1) Two-person registry control: widening changes to principals, targets and verifier keys go through propose_registry_change and approve_registry_change (two distinct persons in two distinct sessions, both registered operators, matching digest, immutable registry_changes and registry_change_approvals tables, REGISTRY_CHANGE_PROPOSED and REGISTRY_CHANGE_APPLIED events, replay-safe, 24 h expiry); the direct register_principal, register_target and register_verifier_key keep their signatures and now accept one operator only for narrowing or exact replay, else TWO_PERSON_REQUIRED; bootstrap: while fewer than two distinct operator persons exist, one operator may add operator or recovery_witness principals only. enforce_registry, revoke_verifier_key and bump_epoch only tighten and stay single-operator (my decision, not a text requirement). (2) Session independence on cosign paths: witness_cosigns and hold_cosigns record signer_session from session_user by trigger; use of a cosign (operator_resolve, operator_close_failed_verify, ANCHOR_GAP release_hold) requires a different person than the signer session and the proposing session; rows without a recorded session cannot be used by a non-superuser (fail closed). (3) Key-lock exclusion via business-key collision: NOT changed; the findings text names no concrete bypass and validate, claim and declare_business_key were read without reproducing one; a pin test c3_pin fixes the verified behaviour. Agent-reported on a disposable container, one file at a time: c 15, digest2 23, b3 26, b2 13, b 14, sweeps 26, registry 62, ledger 69, port 16, dump/restore 6, offline 529 passed; independent re-run pending. Legacy tests adjusted without weakening assertions (arrange_principal and dual helpers because one operator can no longer widen the registry; FILES length assertion). Named limits: two-person strength depends on independent logins and person binding; bootstrap lets one operator add a second person; approver session is not compared in operator_resolve (no column); set_sweep_policy and bump_epoch outside the control; business key hash is requester-supplied; new functions not in the Python port SPEC; session tests use SET LOCAL SESSION AUTHORIZATION, not real network logins. Not gate evidence.
- Batch C review finding fixed (2026-10-14): the independent verifier (reading only) found that one operator could deactivate or strip the operator or recovery_witness capability of another operator through the single-operator narrowing rule of migration 010, drop the operator-person count below two and reopen the bootstrap rule to add new operator or witness persons alone. registry_single_ok now treats such a change of an active operator or witness as widening-equivalent: narrowing must keep the principal active and keep its operator and recovery_witness capabilities, otherwise TWO_PERSON_REQUIRED (also for self-demotion). New test c1_one_operator_cannot_remove_the_second_operator_and_reopen_the_bootstrap; the narrowing test now expects TWO_PERSON_REQUIRED for dropping recovery_witness and uses a narrowing that keeps both capabilities. Evidence: independent run of the 11 batch C files at 8fff7cd: c 15, digest2 23, b3 26, b2 13, b 14, sweeps 26, registry 62, ledger 69, port 16, dump/restore 6, offline 529 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. After the fix, main session re-run on its own container, one file at a time: c 16, b 14, registry 62, dump/restore 6, digest2 23, b3 26, b2 13, sweeps 26, port 16, ledger 69 passed (offline unaffected). Remaining named limit: the operator-person count can still be lowered by a two-person change, which is intended. Not gate evidence.

FACT: 153 functions, all SECURITY DEFINER owned by ledger_owner; PUBLIC executes none and has no schema USAGE; no role memberships; propose_registry_change, approve_registry_change, enforce_registry, register_principal, register_target, register_verifier_key and revoke_verifier_key are executable only by operator_role and ledger_owner; no internal helper, core or trigger function is executable by any runtime role; seven plumbing or pure helpers (caller_role, row_in_scope, set_scope, requested_by_caller, canonical_content_hash, checkpoint_payload, keyed_signature) are executable by the runtime roles by explicit grants in 004, 007 and 008; no runtime role has INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE, REFERENCES or TRIGGER on any change_ledger table or sequence (SELECT only, and none on role_scope, verifier_keys, witness_cosigns, change_budget, verify_attempt_log, approval_envelopes, registry_changes). - S13 event hash v2 as a NON-GATING PROTOTYPE (2026-10-14, operator decision 1, finding B6-m): migration 011_event_hash_v2.sql. The event hash is now change_ledger.event_hash_v2 = sha256('change-ledger-event|v2|' || prev_hash || seq || length-prefixed tenant and company || event_id || change_id || length-prefixed event_type || recorded_at UTC text || payload text), computed by the BEFORE INSERT trigger events_chain_guard (hash_version always 2); verify_chain recomputes by the stored version and reports bad_seq for any other. Existing rows are rehashed once in migration order per (tenant, company) chain after each row is first verified under v1 (a broken chain aborts the whole migration with EVENT_CHAIN_BROKEN_BEFORE_REHASH); immutability is bypassed only inside the migration transaction by DISABLE and ENABLE of the events_immutable trigger; row counts are checked. Checkpoints are immutable and signed over head_hash, so they stay byte-for-byte, get hash_version 1 and stop acting as anchors (issue_permit needs a fresh checkpoint, check_anchor with a v1 hash reports ANCHOR_GAP). Legit only because no real data and no external anchors exist. Tests: 17 new in test_change_hardening_d_sql.py; legacy adjustments without weakening assertions: FILES length assertion, the documented-formula check now uses v2, and the b4 replay test now replays lost events with their event_id and recorded_at (hash covers them). Agent-reported on its own disposable container, one file at a time: d 17, c 16, digest2 23, b3 26, b2 13, b 14, sweeps 26, registry 62, ledger 69, port 16, dump/restore 6, offline 529 passed; independent re-run pending. Named limits: an external anchor recorded as v1 becomes ANCHOR_GAP; an open ANCHOR_GAP hold keeps its v1 ext_head_hash and releases only by the two-person path; a PERMITTED permit needs a fresh checkpoint; restore from an archive must keep event_id and recorded_at; the migration needs the table owner or a superuser; verify_chain accepts v2 only. Not gate evidence.

- S13 batch E: A7-B01, C-M03 and B6-h as a NON-GATING prototype (2026-10-14, design decisions delegated to the main session by the operator): migration 012_dispatch_session_hardening.sql. (1) A7-B01: verify_dispatch_payload (same signature, now VOLATILE) writes an immutable marker per (change, generation, payload_digest) in change_dispatch_verifications plus a DISPATCH_PAYLOAD_VERIFIED event with digests only; the PERMITTED to DISPATCHED step (record_call before dispatch_cas, and a BEFORE UPDATE trigger on dispatch_permits so no other path can reach DISPATCHED) requires a marker for the current generation and payload digest and assert_approval_current; refusals use the existing FORBIDDEN_TRANSITION, APPROVAL_VOIDED and DIGEST_MISMATCH codes and the permit stays PERMITTED. (2) C-M03: approvals.approver_session is filled by a BEFORE INSERT trigger from session_user; the consume guard of 010 now also refuses a consumer in the same person's session as the approver of any APPROVE approval of the change (operator_resolve, operator_close_failed_verify); superuser exempt; an old approval without a recorded session fails closed for a non-superuser on this new comparison only. (3) B6-h: sweep constant verify_min_tick_seconds = 5 with a 1 s floor (set_sweep_policy accepts 1..3600), a SWEEP_STUCK tick closer than the minimum to the previous counted attempt is not counted (throttled), so interval 0 cannot burn the bound. Tests: 17 new in test_change_hardening_e_sql.py; legacy adjustments without weakening assertions: FILES length assertions, L.verify_dispatch and the port dispatch helper call verify_dispatch_payload before record_call, d old_world uses FILES[:10], and two sweep tests that burned the bound by back-to-back sweeps now age the counter between sweeps. Agent-reported on its own disposable container, one file at a time: e 17, d 17, c 16, digest2 23, b3 26, b2 13, b 14, sweeps 26, registry 62, ledger 69, port 16, dump/restore 6, offline 529 passed; independent re-run pending. Named limits: the ledger proves that verification of the approved payload was requested before dispatch, not that the bytes sent to 1C equal the verified bytes; A7-B02 and B6-g still have no data source; verify_inconclusive attempts are counted per evidence, not per tick; T12 and LOCK_BUSY timers unchanged; offline model unchanged. Not gate evidence.
- Independent verification of S13 batch E (2026-10-14, HEAD 37b4d39): a separate e2e-runner agent ran 13 focused files one at a time on its own disposable container (removed): e 17, d 17, c 16, digest2 23, b3 26, b2 13, b 14, sweeps 26, registry 62, ledger 69, port 16, dump/restore 6, offline 529 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped. Reading only (no guard broken): (a) no function or role-level path reaches DISPATCHED without a marker for the current generation and payload digest (the BEFORE UPDATE OF state trigger on dispatch_permits covers dispatch_cas, direct UPDATE and any other function); residual privilege-dependent gaps: an INSERT of a permit already in DISPATCHED is not guarded (all inserting functions use the default state), a superuser or table owner can disable the trigger, a direct UPDATE of records.dispatch_state is not guarded in the SQL (runtime roles hold SELECT only per the live ACL check of f409c37, to be re-checked after 012). (b) The approver-session guard covers every consume of witness_cosigns; limits: hold_cosigns has no approver comparison by design, only APPROVE rows are compared, when the registry is not enforced and a role has no person mapping same_person falls back to string equality, superuser exempt. (c) the 1 s floor is enforced for every caller (set_sweep_policy range check, greatest(1, ...), recover_sweep overrides the caller value); MINOR: a direct write of a non-numeric verify_min_tick_seconds into sweep_policy.params would make the cast raise (owner-level access). (d) wiring block last and identical to 011. Not gate evidence.
- S13 migration 013 as a NON-GATING prototype (2026-10-15, closes GPT-PM F-02 and the E-03/E-04 conditions of review 5b8d2f17): 013_dispatched_invariant.sql. (1) BEFORE INSERT trigger on dispatch_permits refuses any state other than NOT_PERMITTED. (2) BEFORE UPDATE trigger on records (fires when dispatch_state changes): DISPATCHED requires the permit of the current claim generation to be DISPATCHED and assert_dispatch_verified (012 function) to accept it, with claim_generation and payload_digest unchanged in the same statement; PERMITTED requires a PERMITTED permit of the current generation; no DISPATCHED to PERMITTED fall-back. (3) BEFORE INSERT trigger on records refuses a non-default dispatch_state. Addition beyond the brief: the 012 permit guard now allows DISPATCHED only from PERMITTED and makes DISPATCHED terminal for a permit (012 let NOT_PERMITTED or CANCELLED_UNDISPATCHED reach DISPATCHED once a marker existed, skipping issue_permit, checkpoint, TTL and fence). Triggers are not role based, so they stop owner and superuser plain DML; they do not defend against DISABLE TRIGGER, DROP TRIGGER, CREATE OR REPLACE of the guards, session_replication_role=replica or a data-only restore into a schema with existing triggers. Moving a permit to PERMITTED is not guarded. Live ACL re-verification after 001-013 on a fresh database (read-only, agent-run): 161 functions, all SECURITY DEFINER owned by ledger_owner with pinned search_path, none executable by PUBLIC, 93 executable by no runtime role, runtime roles have explicit grants only and no membership in each other or superuser; no runtime role has INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE, TRUNCATE, REFERENCES or TRIGGER on any of 40 relations (column level and sequences included); record_call executable only by writer_role, verify_dispatch_payload by writer_role and requester_role, set_sweep_policy by operator_role, event_hash_v2 by auditor_role; seven plumbing or pure helpers (caller_role, row_in_scope, canonical_content_hash, checkpoint_payload, keyed_signature, requested_by_caller, set_scope) are explicitly granted to all runtime roles. Not covered: a live deployed schema and the clt_* test logins. Tests: 11 new in test_change_hardening_f_sql.py; two FILES-length assertions changed from 12 to 13. Agent-reported on its own disposable container, one file at a time: f 11, e 17, d 17, c 16, digest2 23, b3 26, b2 13, b 14, sweeps 26, registry 62, ledger 69, port 16, dump/restore 6, offline 529 passed; independent re-run pending. Not gate evidence. # 2026-10-15 — S14-R03 synthetic dispatch coordinator contract

Decision: implement a reusable coordinator only inside the synthetic simulation package. It will
sequence the existing SQL receipt requirement, canonical payload verification and call recording
before `ReferenceWriter`, then persist the unchanged clone receipt only after `APPLIED_VERIFIED`.
Already-issued IDs are inputs, not proof of authority. The coordinator will not mint approvals,
claims, permits, tickets or signatures; it will not complete changes, retry writes, implement
`OneCWritePort`, or claim real-system qualification. Canonical bytes continue to come from the
existing serializer, and SQL remains authoritative for approval and receipt checks.

Rationale: the combined end-to-end scenario currently spells this safe sequence inline, while
`SqlChangeLedger` lacks public wrappers for the two receipt SQL functions. Encapsulating that exact
sequence reduces scenario duplication without broadening the trust boundary. Unknown outcomes remain
unknown and are returned for the established recovery path.

Evidence/state: design-first contract at `docs/phase2/S14_SYNTHETIC_DISPATCH_ADAPTER_2026-10-15.md`.
Implementation, focused tests, specialist review and GPT-PM exact-head decision are pending. This
prototype does not change G0-G8 (NOT_PASSED), formal S13 entry (NOT APPROVED), or Release 2 (NO-GO).

## 2026-10-15 — S14-R03 synthetic dispatch coordinator implementation

Implemented `SyntheticDispatchCoordinator` in the synthetic simulation package and added scoped
`SqlChangeLedger` wrappers for existing SQL receipt requirement/recording functions. The coordinator
uses exact canonical serializer bytes, runs ledger gates before the one writer call, persists only
the unmodified receipt after `APPLIED_VERIFIED`, and neither completes nor retries. False gate
results stop before the write. This is not `OneCWritePort` and does not issue authorization artifacts.

Verification state: coding is complete; focused functional/E2E agent tests and specialist review are
pending. See `docs/phase2/CHANGE_RECORD_036.md`. G0-G8 remain NOT_PASSED, formal S13 entry NOT
APPROVED, and Release 2 NO-GO.

Focused test evidence (2026-10-15): `functional-test-reviewer` added and ran
`python -m unittest tests.phase2.test_synthetic_dispatch -v` with `PYTHONPATH=src`: 5/5 passed.
`e2e-runner` ran `tests/phase2/test_simulation_e2e_sql.py` on disposable PostgreSQL 16: 18/18 passed
in 109.77s. The SQL wrapper success scenario persisted the exact clone receipt before APPLIED;
crash-before/after-write remained APPLYING with zero receipt and one clone call. An initial setup
run failed because the test registry lacked the clone's actual receipt key; the fixture was corrected
and the full file passed on rerun. This is test-lane evidence only. Specialist review and exact-head
GPT-PM review are pending. Agent cleanup of disposable container
`s14-r03-e2e-c4b4d32f9e39` was rejected before execution; it remains running on localhost port 27780.

Review correction: the Python reviewer found that a SQL receipt-persistence failure after
`APPLIED_VERIFIED` could resemble a pre-write refusal. The fix raises typed
`ReceiptPersistenceUnknown` with the verified clone outcome; callers must reconcile SQL state and
must not retry because SQL may have committed before the response was lost. A second focused review
found the cancellation wrapper suppressed normal task-cancellation semantics. The fix is to surface
`ReceiptPersistenceCancelled`, a subclass of `asyncio.CancelledError` carrying the verified outcome;
the functional file passed 7/7 with actual task cancellation during a suspended SQL await, asserting
cancelled task state, error code, result preservation and one writer call. The E2E file passed 18/18
on rerun at source HEAD `d659788`. Independent reviews found no BLOCKER/MAJOR; the task-lifecycle
coverage gap is closed. Exact-head GPT-PM review remains pending. See
`docs/phase2/CHANGE_RECORD_038.md` and `CHANGE_RECORD_039.md`.

## 2026-10-21 - Operator decisions A1-A7 recorded for autonomous ERP_MCP work

Evidence basis: `docs/phase2/AUTONOMOUS_EXECUTION_PLAN_2026-10-21.md` records the operator statements and the agent's repository/host verification. This entry preserves the limits from that verification; a recorded operator statement is not upgraded into runtime evidence.

- A1 — Source availability: operator statement, “A 1C database exists on this computer and on the remote computer, so real-source access is not a blocker.” WP0 observed the local listeners, anonymous-access denial, gateway `/readyz` and `/healthz` responses, and IdP JWKS reachability (`docs/phase2/artifacts/WP0_SOURCE_LIVENESS_2026-10-21.md`). The local business read remains NOT_RUN. Ivan remote checks also remain NOT_RUN because strict SSH host-key verification rejected the untrusted host key. Local liveness does not establish a successful business read or remote source health.
- A2 — G3 accountant report: operator statement, “The independent accountant report (gate G3) is not a blocker; the report does not exist, but everything is fine.” Decision: record `WAIVED_BY_OPERATOR` as an unsigned G3 note only. Engineering reconciliation is not accountant attestation. G3 remains `NOT_PASSED`; the note cannot satisfy predecessors, authorize production, or be reported as PASS. No accountant report was found in the cited plan evidence.
- A3 — Key registry: operator statement, “Key and signature registry (risks R-01, R-03): permitted.” This authorizes the planned implementation only; it is not evidence that keys, signers, or witnesses exist. Before the first real write, the plan still requires the actual licensed-host signer and separate witness, with private keys protected and public keys registered.
- A4 — Donor licenses: operator statement, “Donor-code licenses are not needed: it is the operator’s own code from other projects.” This closes the donor-code approval item as an operator decision. It does not close the inventory's unverified transitive dependency, setuptools/wheel, or License-File checks; no complete license audit is claimed.
- A5 — Google Drive OAuth: operator statement, “Google Drive OAuth: create, delete and read of documents were already fully verified.” Repository records cited by the plan support create, read/list and changes-feed probes, but no delete probe was found. Keep Drive intake disabled and outside the critical path until its scope, token lifetime and evidence are resolved; do not claim delete verification.
- A6 — Secrets, canary and rollback: operator delegated the choices with “decide yourself.” Decision: use the existing per-host Windows DPAPI CurrentUser-to-environment (`BAG_SECRET_PROVIDER=env`) arrangement; a read-only recipe on `onec-818ha-reference` via the Ivan gateway, with a two-hour window, gateway row limit 200 and 100 calls per run; use the S13 kill-switch design. Operator remains accountable for rollback, executed through that kill switch. Staging remains forbidden per the recorded 2026-10-10 operator decision. These are selected operating parameters, not evidence of a canary run.
- A7 — 1C writes: operator statement, “you have everything”; follow-up: “on production there will be no tests, an exact clone is enough.” Authorization is limited to disposable test clones / the named lane copy. Production company bases are never test targets; no production base write is authorized. CT14 evidence covers only the measured COM purchase-receipt create/post/unpost path on `818HA_p2diff`; the plan lists unmeasured OData, forced termination, concurrent unresolved-call and client/server-base cases.

G3 manifest mechanics: `governance/GATE_MANIFEST.json` may carry a structured `operator_disposition` note with status `WAIVED_BY_OPERATOR`, statement and recording date. The validator accepts this only on G3, rejects signature/signer/evidence fields on the note, and never treats it as gate evidence. G3's gate status and external evidence status remain unchanged. G4 retains its separate signed-waiver mechanics. Tests for this change are owned by the named gate-manifest test agents and were not run by this implementation pass.

## 2026-10-22 - Preserve localized and semantic text during WP3 cleanup

Decision: preserve Russian-localized documentation, Russian semantic/test strings, and literal 1C identifiers rather than deleting them or translating values that define behavior. For future English-text scanning, do not grant an entire mixed source/test-data directory a broad exception based only on the path-based inventory. Build exceptions from exact paths and reviewed line/pattern scope; keep prose outside those scopes subject to translation. The 2026-10-20 inventory remains a historical heuristic, not line-level proof. No scanner, test, or current allowlist is approved by this decision.

Authority: the operator delegated routine technical decisions to Codex in the autonomous ERP_MCP task; this choice preserves product/test semantics and avoids deletion. Evidence: `docs/commits/2026-10-20-english-policy-inventory.md` records the localization, literal-identifier, and semantic-data classes as exception candidates and warns that classification is path-based. The precise allowlist remains open until a value-safe line-level inventory is available.

For direct operator quotations in this decision log, preserve short verbatim wording in its original language for audit fidelity and translate only the surrounding narrative. Any future English-text scanner exception must be anchored to the specific quoted text in a value-safe way; do not exempt the whole decision-log file. This applies the operator's broad delegation of ordinary policy decisions while keeping attribution exact.

The design proposes one executor, a rolled-back clone probe first, and lists five open decisions; the reference format of the five payload references blocks code. Detail: `docs/phase2/S14_COM_DRAFT_EXECUTOR_DESIGN_2026-10-27.md`, `docs/phase2/CHANGE_RECORD_378.md`.

## 2026-10-27 - COM CREATE_DRAFT executor implemented against fake COM objects; references are resolved as GUIDs

Decision: the executor refuses before the write call with outcome_unknown false and reports outcome_unknown true when the write or its read-back fails; posting is never referenced. The reference-format question was auto-answered (unique identifier) while the operator was silent and can be reverted. 52 agent-run tests passed on fakes; behavior against real 1C is UNKNOWN until the clone probe. Detail: `docs/phase2/CHANGE_RECORD_379.md`.

## 2026-10-27 - COM draft executor hardened; real-clone probes found a silent-failure class that fakes and reviews missed

Decision: assign enumeration values as enumeration objects and read back references, enumerations and markers from the stored document; the document's own date is a required argument; the includes-VAT flag is a reviewed constant. Real 1C stores an enumeration assigned as text as EMPTY without an error. The real executor then ran on the disposable clone in a rolled-back transaction (OK, total 20, 0 leftovers); tests 319 passed. Detail: `docs/phase2/CHANGE_RECORD_380.md`.

## 2026-10-27 - COM draft executor handles dates through 1C values; least-privilege role gets Read on four catalogs

Decision: dates are assigned only as 1C date values built by the type-description conversion and compared as text; the document's own date is compared by calendar day only. The role CT14 writer got Read on four catalogs (operator silent; the recommended option was auto-accepted and is revertible). The executor ran OK on the clone as administrator and as the narrow user (rolled back). Detail: `docs/phase2/CHANGE_RECORD_381.md`.

## 2026-10-27 - Executor fixes from two independent reviews; document time-of-day measured

Decision: accept the reviews' fixes (pre-write type and date checks, document reference kept after late failures, posted and deletion-mark read-back, case-insensitive names). Rejected one concern: the stored time of the document date is always 07:00:00 for six assigned times, so no midnight crossing exists and the day-only comparison stays. Executor ran OK on the clone as administrator and narrow user (rolled back). Detail: `docs/phase2/CHANGE_RECORD_382.md`.

Decision: accept the finding; identity text is compared as exact text, only declared number paths are compared numerically, and the empty-text-equals-None rule is removed. Executor ran OK on the clone as administrator and narrow user after the change (rolled back). Detail: `docs/phase2/CHANGE_RECORD_383.md`.
